- Integration tests (tests/room_list.rs, verbatim ignore reason): `search_query_filters_the_list`, `tab_queries_split_dms_rooms_and_spaces` (adds a plain room so the Rooms tab is non-empty), `load_more_rooms_adds_a_page` (page size 2 through the new `common::start_core_with_page_size`; waits for `RoomListLoaded` before `LoadMoreRooms`).
- Open issues: the local Synapse became very slow for room creation as test data piled up (one `create_dm` + `create_space` + `create_room` took about 3 minutes; the whole `room_list` file took 220 s, then 790 s). The tab test failed once with a 30 s timeout in a full-file run, then passed alone and in the next full run (not twice in a row). If it recurs, reset the Synapse volume (`docker compose down -v`).
- Flaky integration run, root cause (two independent causes, both test infrastructure; core code unchanged):
  1. Slowness: Synapse's default rate limits. `docker compose logs synapse` showed `SynapseError: 429 - Too Many Requests (rc_room_creation)` on `createRoom` and `(rc_messages)` on message sends for `@alice` (defaults: 10 room creations then 1 per minute). The SDK honours `retry_after` and sleeps, so a create took up to about a minute each (hence the minutes-long runs and the earlier 30 s timeout of `tab_queries_split_dms_rooms_and_spaces`). Fix: `dev/synapse/start.sh` sets `rc_message`, `rc_room_creation`, `rc_joins`, `rc_invites`, `rc_3pid_validation` to 1000/s (local test server only), appended once behind a `# tessera-dev-rate-limits` marker so an existing volume gets it on the next container start too. After the fix the logs show 0 `Too Many` lines.
  2. Deterministic failure of `dm_and_space_flags` (the 30 s timeout, even with no 429s): `Client::create_dm` marks the room in `m.direct` by reading the client's local copy and PUTting the whole event. The test helper clients never sync, so that copy is empty and each DM replaced the server's `m.direct`; `dm_and_space_flags` and `tab_queries_split_dms_rooms_and_spaces` run concurrently on the same account (alice to bob), so one test's DM was dropped from `m.direct` and stayed `is_dm == false`. Passed alone (5 s), failed in the full file. Fix: `create_dm` in `tests/common/mod.rs` runs under a process-wide lock, fetches `m.direct` from the server first and writes the merged event back after creating the DM. No assertion or timeout changed.
  - Possible product note (not changed, not this step): a room list `Set` is not emitted when `m.direct` arrives after the room, so a DM seen before its `m.direct` stays non-DM until the list is rebuilt. Worth a look in a later step.
- Verification of the fix: ignored run (`cargo test -p tessera-core --locked -- --ignored --skip keyring`) three times in a row, run 1 on a fresh `docker compose down -v` volume; all passed. `room_list` file wall time: 11.14 s, 12.35 s, 14.12 s (was 190 s). Before the fix on a fresh volume: 189 s, failing.
- No new human items.

## Phase 2 / Task 5 notes
- Checks: `cargo fmt --all --check`, clippy (`--workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -D warnings`), `cargo test --workspace --locked`, `scripts/check-app-deps.sh`, `scripts/check-core-api.sh`, `cargo deny check` and the ignored run (`cargo test -p tessera-core --locked -- --ignored --skip keyring`) all pass (all suites ok at that commit; the integration suites were avatars 1 passed in 8.5 s, room_list 8 in 17 s). The keyring test was not run.
- TDD: the first 11 `avatars.rs` unit tests were written against `todo!()` stubs and failed (11 failed, 1 passed), then the code was added; later security rounds raised the module to 18 unit tests (see the follow-up notes below).
- Core: private module `avatars.rs` (not in `check-core-api.sh`). `Active` owns `avatars: Avatars` (a `JoinSet`, the seen set, a 4-permit `Semaphore`) and `fetcher: Arc<SdkFetcher>`; `stop_tasks` calls `avatars.stop()` (abort all, drain, clear the seen set) first, before the sync tasks and the store go. With no session, `FetchAvatars` answers every ID with `image: None` (`answer_unavailable`).
- Security rules from the reviews, all covered by unit tests:
  - Each `AvatarId` is parsed as an `OwnedMxcUri` and must be `mxc://server/media` (non-empty media ID, at most 255 bytes) before any request. `"not-mxc"`, `https://` and `file://` URLs, userinfo, `/`, `?`, `#`, `..`, whitespace, wrong case of the scheme, empty media ID and over-long IDs give `image: None` with zero fetch calls. The 255-byte bound also avoids a wrap in ruma's validator (`(index + 6) as u8` then `NonZeroU8::new(..).unwrap()`, ruma-identifiers-validation mxc_uri.rs:25, which could panic for a 250-byte server name).
  - Fetching is only through `client.media().get_media_content(&MediaRequestParameters { source: MediaSource::Plain(parsed), format }, true)`. The `Fetcher` trait takes the parsed `OwnedMxcUri`, so no URL is ever built from the raw string.
  - Cap: 1 MiB (inclusive) on the thumbnail; empty is unavailable; an oversized thumbnail is refused. There is no `File` fallback (removed in the security follow-up below). Residual: the SDK's media API returns a whole `Vec<u8>` (no streaming limit exists in 0.19.1: no response-size option in `RequestConfig`), so the cap applies after the download and the SDK has already stored it in its encrypted media cache; the bytes are not handed on. Memory for one download is bounded only by the server.
  - A command looks at no more than 256 IDs (`bounded_unique`: order kept, repeats removed, the rest ignored and not answered; the app asks again for what it still renders). Every answer is sent from a task, so the command loop never waits on the network or on a full event channel.
- Behaviour: an ID in flight or already answered gets no second event (the second `FetchAvatars([id])` gives none); a repeat of an ID within one command is dropped too, so each ID gets exactly one answer per session. At most 4 fetches run at once (permit held for the fetch only). The thumbnail is 96x96 with `use_cache = true`; the original file is never requested. SDK error text is dropped (it can quote server data); image bytes are never logged (`AvatarImage` Debug shows the length).
- Fixture: `crates/core/tests/fixtures/avatar.png` is a 64x64 8-bit RGB PNG (7858 bytes) generated for this step with a short Python script (zlib and struct only, a plain gradient); it is original, with no third-party content or licence.
- Integration test (`tests/avatars.rs`, verbatim ignore reason): alice uploads the fixture as a new room's avatar through `Room::upload_avatar`; the core's list shows `avatar: Some(id)`; `FetchAvatars([not-mxc, id, id])` gives one `None` and one `Some(bytes)` (non-empty) within 30 s; a second `FetchAvatars([id])` gives no event within 3 s; then the tempdir is scanned: any file containing the returned bytes or the fixture must be a SQLite file (`.sqlite` in its name).
- APIs used, confirmed in `~/.cargo/registry/src/index.crates.io-1949cf8c6b5b557f/` (pinned versions):
  - matrix-sdk 0.19.1: `Client::media` client/mod.rs:928; `Media::get_media_content` media.rs:431 (cache read/write inside, :431-480); `Room::upload_avatar` room/mod.rs:3040 (tests).
  - matrix-sdk-base 0.19.1 media/mod.rs: `MediaFormat` :46, `MediaThumbnailSettings` :65 with `new(width, height)` :99, `MediaRequestParameters` :130, `MediaSource::Plain` (re-exported by ruma events::room).
  - ruma-common 0.20.0 identifiers/mxc_uri.rs: `MxcUri::parts` :35, `is_valid` :51; ruma-identifiers-validation `mxc_uri::validate` :7.
  - tokio 1.53.1: `JoinSet::spawn` task/join_set.rs:142, `join_next` :296, `try_join_next` :323, `abort_all` :463; `Arc<Semaphore>::acquire_owned` sync/semaphore.rs:796.
  - `uint!` is re-exported through `matrix_sdk::ruma` (compiled). `mime` is not re-exported by the SDK, so the test passes `&"image/png".parse().unwrap()` (inferred as `Mime`) and adds no dependency.
- The CLI is unchanged (it ignores `Avatar`). Nothing is cached outside the SDK store.
- Security follow-up (audit of bb42bd3), deviation from plan Q11 ("fall back to `MediaFormat::File` only if the thumbnail request fails"): the fallback to the original file is removed; only the server-generated 96x96 thumbnail is requested. Reason: in matrix-sdk 0.19.1 `DefaultMediaFetcher` sets `.timeout(Some(Duration::MAX))` (media.rs:818) and reads the whole body into memory, and `get_media_content(.., true)` caches it before our size check, so a room with a huge non-image avatar could buffer and cache large downloads and hold all 4 permits forever. The SDK offers no per-request media timeout (the fetcher builds its own `RequestConfig`), so every fetch is wrapped in `tokio::time::timeout` with `FETCH_DEADLINE` = 20 s (the timed-out future is dropped, which releases the permit). Tests: `thumbnail_error_is_unavailable_and_the_file_is_never_requested`, `a_fetch_that_never_completes_times_out`, `hanging_fetches_release_their_permits` (real short deadline, never-completing fake; no tokio `test-util`). Residual: a thumbnail request is still unbounded in size if a malicious server ignores the thumbnail parameters and returns a huge body, but only until the 20 s deadline.
- Local media: `parse_avatar_id` now refuses server names `*.localhost` (case-insensitive, port ignored), because `Media::is_local_uri` (media.rs:792, `LOCAL_MXC_SERVER_NAME = "send-queue.localhost"` :63) serves `mxc://send-queue.localhost/<txn>` from the local store (the user's pending uploads). The bare `localhost` (the local test homeserver) stays valid.
- Retry: a fetch that returns nothing (error, timeout, empty, oversized) removes its ID from the shared `seen` set before the `None` answer is sent, so a later `FetchAvatars` retries it; in-flight and successful IDs stay deduplicated; invalid IDs are not retried. `seen` is capped at `MAX_SEEN` = 4096 and cleared when full.
- Open issues: none new. No new human items beyond the checkpoint item below.

## Phase 2 / Task 6 notes
- Checks: `cargo fmt --all --check`, clippy (`--workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -D warnings`), `cargo test --workspace --locked` (app: 16 lib tests, 5 bin tests), `scripts/check-app-deps.sh`, `scripts/check-core-api.sh`, `cargo deny check` and the ignored run (`cargo test -p tessera-core -p tessera-app --locked -- --ignored --skip keyring`) all pass (app `tests/bridge.rs` 1 passed in 4.8 s; all core suites ok). `docker compose stop` afterwards. The keyring test was not run. actionlint is not installed here: the only CI change is the one `cargo test` line of the `integration` job (Q16), not run through actionlint.
- TDD: the `state` tests (11) were written against a `todo!()` `update` and the two stream tests against an empty stream; 11 failed, then the code was added. The integration test then exposed a bug the unit tests missed (`Bridge::deadline` built `tokio::time::sleep` on the UI thread, which has no runtime context: "there is no reactor running"); a regression test `timer_can_be_started_from_a_thread_without_a_runtime` was written, seen to fail against the old code, and the `sleep` is now created inside the spawned task (`spawn_timer`).
- App: `crates/app` is now a lib (`bridge`, `state`) plus the `tessera` bin. `main.rs` parses `--data-dir`, `--homeserver`, `--ephemeral`, `--help` (Q20 note in the help text), calls `logging::init("warn,tessera_core=info,tessera_app=info")`, and runs `iced::application(App::booter(startup), App::update, App::view)` with `.title`, `.subscription`, `.theme`, `.exit_on_close_request(false)`. Deviation from the plan text: the boot function is `App::booter(Startup)` (a closure that takes the `Startup` once) instead of the bare `App::boot`, because boot needs the already started bridge and Iced's `BootFn` is a zero-argument `Fn`.
- `bridge.rs`: `Bridge::start(config, secrets) -> io::Result<(Bridge, EventsSlot)>` (the runtime build can fail), `Bridge::send(&self, Command) -> Option<Task<Message>>` (`try_send`; a full channel gives a `Pending` whose task does the channel `send`; a closed channel drops the command), `Bridge::deadline(Duration) -> Task<Message>` (timer on the core runtime, Q18), `events_stream`, `events_subscription(slot)`, `shutdown_timeout(5 s)` in `Drop`.
- `state.rs`: `App` holds a `Box<dyn CommandSink>` (`Bridge` in production, `mpsc::Sender<Command>` in tests, no runtime). Boot sends `RestoreSession` and returns `system::theme().map(Message::ThemeMode)`. `Screen` is `Starting | Login | Loading | Main | ShuttingDown | Disconnected`. `CloseRequested` sends `Shutdown` once and shows `ShuttingDown`; `CoreClosed` (or the 10 s `ShutdownDeadline`) while shutting down sets `exit_requested` and returns `iced::exit()`; `CoreClosed` otherwise shows `Disconnected`. `RoomListChanged` is applied with `ListDiff::apply`; the first list diff or `RoomListLoaded` moves `Loading` to `Main` (Tasks 7 and 9 refine this). `LoginSubmitted` carries an `api::Password` (redacted `Debug`, tested with a sentinel). The theme is plain `Theme::Light`/`Dark` from the mode; Task 8 adds the palettes. `ThemeMode` from `system::theme_changes()` is not subscribed yet (Task 8).
- The "full capacity-1 sink" test checks that `update` returns within 50 ms, that the fallback task is non-empty (`Task::units() > 0`) and that nothing but the boot command is queued; the delivery itself is tested on `Pending::deliver` directly, because `iced::runtime::task::into_stream` is not reachable from the app (iced re-exports `iced_runtime` only partially) and a `Task` is opaque.
- Integration test (`tests/bridge.rs`, verbatim ignore reason): ensures alice has a room through a few raw HTTP/1.0 requests to the local homeserver (login, `createRoom`, logout; no new dependency), starts a real `Bridge` with an in-memory secret store and a scratch data dir, pumps the slot's stream into `App::update`, logs in, waits for `Screen::Main` with at least one room (60 s), sends `CloseRequested`, and waits for `exit_requested` (the `CoreClosed` path, 10 s). The test creates one room per run on the local server and leaves one device session of alice (as the CLI tests do).
- `--ephemeral` removes its temp dir on exit but does not revoke the session on the server (the CLI does); stated in `--help`.
- APIs used, confirmed in `~/.cargo/registry/src/index.crates.io-1949cf8c6b5b557f/` (iced 0.14.0, tokio 1.53.1):
  - iced: `application` application.rs:79; `BootFn` :535 (any `Fn() -> impl IntoBoot`); `Application::title` :346, `subscription` :363, `theme` :378, `exit_on_close_request` :269, `run` :186; `Task::{none, future :258, batch, map, discard :222, units :282}` (iced_runtime task.rs); `iced::exit` (iced_runtime lib.rs:124, re-exported iced lib.rs:542); `iced::system::{theme, theme_changes}` (iced lib.rs:602-605; iced_runtime system.rs:58/:63); `theme::Mode` (iced_core theme.rs:212, `iced::theme` lib.rs:533); `window::close_requests` (iced_runtime window.rs:260, through `iced::window` window.rs `pub use runtime::window::*`); `Subscription::run_with` (iced_futures subscription.rs:198) and `Subscription::map` (:282, non-capturing closure only); `iced::futures` (lib.rs:484) with `stream::unfold` (futures-util unfold.rs:50), `StreamExt::boxed` (futures-util stream/stream/mod.rs:1334) and `executor::block_on` (futures lib.rs:150, used in unit tests).
  - tokio: `runtime::Builder::{new_multi_thread :276, enable_all :376, thread_name :594, build :1072}`, `Runtime::{enter :419, shutdown_timeout :452}`, `Handle::spawn` (runtime/handle.rs:197), `mpsc::Sender::{try_send :924, send :816}`, `TrySendError::{Full, Closed}` (sync/mpsc/error.rs:28), `Receiver::recv` (bounded.rs:243), `time::sleep` (time/sleep.rs:123).

- Checks: `cargo fmt --all --check`, clippy (`--workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -D warnings`), `cargo test --workspace --locked` (app: 77 lib tests, 5 bin tests), `scripts/check-app-deps.sh`, `scripts/check-core-api.sh` and `cargo deny check` all pass. The keyring test was not run. No window was opened and no browser was started.

The 20 new `state` tests were written against `todo!()` update arms (20 failed), then the code was added. Not seen red: the small pure helpers `last_messages`, `sync_label`, `room_count_label` and the views (they were written together with their tests).
- App: new `screens/` (`mod.rs` with `centered_text`, `login.rs`, `loading.rs`, `main_view.rs`). `Screen` gains `WaitingForBrowser`. `Message::LoginSubmitted{..}` is reshaped (Task 6 open issue): `ServerChanged`, `UsernameChanged`, `PasswordChanged(Password)`, unit `LoginSubmitted`, `OAuthRequested`, `CancelOAuth`, `BrowserOpened{ok}`, `CopyLink`, `ComposerChanged(MessageText)`, `ComposerSubmitted`, `LogoutRequested`. `App` gains `login: LoginForm`, `login_in_flight`, `notice` (replaces `login_error`), `browser_link`, `browser_opened`, `composer: MessageText`, `send_error`, and a manual `Debug` (screen, counts, login form, composer; never rooms, timeline bodies or the sink). `tests/bridge.rs` now types the form and sends `LoginSubmitted`.
- Transitions (all unit-tested): boot `Starting`; `NoSession` to `Login`; `LoggedIn` to `Loading` (also when restored); `Loading` to `Main` on `RoomListLoaded` or a non-empty list (an empty first diff no longer opens `Main`); `LoginFailed(e)` to `Login` with `e.to_string()` as the notice (`Cancelled` shows "Sign-in cancelled.") and the password cleared; `LoggedOut` / `SessionExpired` to `Login` with different notices; `OAuthUrl` to `WaitingForBrowser`; Cancel sends `CancelOAuth` and the core's `LoginFailed(Cancelled)` returns to `Login`.
- Login: Enter or **Log in** needs a non-empty user name and password (else a notice, nothing sent). The server is trimmed, empty means `None`. On submit the password is moved out of the form into `Command::LoginPassword` (`mem::replace`) and the field is cleared; `login_in_flight` blocks every second submit (password or OIDC) and the widgets lose their `on_input`/`on_press` while it is set. **Sign in with browser** needs a non-blank server. A password field keeps the `Password` type end to end (the widget reads `expose()`); iced's own text-input buffer is not zeroized (known residual, Q19).
- OIDC (Q21 plus the security review): `screens::login::check_browser_url(&str) -> Result<BrowserUrl, UrlRefused>` is pure and has no URL crate (the app may only depend on core, iced, image, tokio, open). It requires pure ASCII with no whitespace, control characters or backslash; scheme `https`, or `http` only when the host is `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]` or `localhost` (case-insensitive); no `@` in the authority (userinfo); a plain DNS-label, IPv4 or bracketed-IPv6 host; an optional numeric port within u16. `UrlRefused` (`Malformed`, `Scheme`, `Userinfo`, `InsecureHost`) never carries the URL. `BrowserUrl` is the only type `open_in_browser` takes, and its `Debug` is redacted. Tests: https and loopback http allowed; other http, `127.0.0.2`, `localhost.evil.com`, `127.0.0.1.evil.com`, IPv4-mapped IPv6 refused; userinfo variants incl. `http://127.0.0.1@evil.com`; `javascript:`, `data:`, `file:`, `ftp:`, scheme-less and empty refused; ~25 malformed forms; no error or `Debug` shows a sentinel `state`.
- Opening the browser: `App::on_oauth_url` runs `check_browser_url` first and only for a login that is in flight; a refused URL gives a notice without the URL, sends `CancelOAuth` and returns no task (tested with `units() == 0`). An allowed URL returns `Task::perform(async move { open_in_browser(&link) }, BrowserOpened)`. `update` never calls `open`: `open_in_browser` lives in `screens/login.rs`, and a test checks that the production part of `state.rs` has no `open::` or `that_detached`. The URL is never logged or displayed: the waiting screen shows "Continue in your browser" with **Cancel**, and **Copy link** (`clipboard::write` of the checked URL) only when the launcher failed.
- Loading screen: "Sync: Running / Offline / Reconnecting (attempt n) ..." and "n rooms loaded" (pure `sync_label`, `room_count_label`, tested).
- Main layout: fixed 300 px left pane (a placeholder list of the first 100 rooms as plain buttons that send `SelectRoom`; Task 10 replaces it with the windowed list), right pane with the selected room's name, the last 50 `Message` items as `sender: body` (`last_messages`, tested), `SendFailed` line, and a single-line composer (`MessageText`; no `on_input`, so disabled, with no selected room). Enter sends `SendMessage` and clears the field; blank text sends nothing. Selecting another room, deselecting and logging out clear the draft and the send error. A status bar shows the sync state, the account and **Log out** (`Command::Logout`).
- Redaction tests: `Debug` of `PasswordChanged("hunter2-x")`, `ComposerChanged("secret-body-x")`, `LoginForm` and the whole `App` after typing both contain neither sentinel.
- APIs used, confirmed in `~/.cargo/registry/src/index.crates.io-1949cf8c6b5b557f/` (iced 0.14.0, iced_widget 0.14.2, open 5.4.4); everything also compiled under clippy:
  - open-5.4.4 src/lib.rs:285 `that_detached(impl AsRef<OsStr>) -> io::Result<()>`.
  - iced_runtime-0.14.0: `clipboard::write<T>(String) -> Task<T>` src/clipboard.rs:49 (re-exported iced lib.rs:563-567); `Task::perform` src/task.rs:48.
  - iced_widget-0.14.2: `text_input(placeholder, value)` helpers.rs:1444, `secure` text_input.rs:163, `on_input_maybe` :184, `on_submit_maybe` :201; `button` helpers.rs:1074, `on_press_maybe` button.rs:170, `button::secondary` :613, `button::text` :673; `scrollable` helpers.rs:1046, `anchor_bottom` scrollable.rs:175; `container` helpers.rs:237, `center_x`/`center_y` container.rs:145/:150; `space()` helpers.rs:1748 with `width` space.rs:44; `Column::{spacing, max_width, align_x}` column.rs:97/:121/:127, `Row::{spacing, align_y}` row.rs:94/:118; `column(iter)` helpers.rs:480 and the `column!`/`row!` macros :60/:94.
  - iced_core-0.14.0: `text::danger` widget/text.rs:473, `Text::{center :126, size :83, style :160, width :114}`, `text::Style { color }` :396; `Length::Fixed` length.rs:22; `iced::{Center, Fill}` iced lib.rs:545-546.
- Open issues: (1) fixed, see the review fixes below. (2) iced's text-input buffers are not zeroized (Q19 residual). (3) `open_in_browser` runs on iced's executor; on Linux `xdg-open` starts quickly, but a slow launcher would block one executor thread. (4) `http://localhost` is allowed per the review even though a hosts file could point it elsewhere. (5) `Core` emits `OAuthUrl` only after `LoginOAuth`; if a core ever sent one unsolicited, the app ignores it.
- New human items (rule 9, not ticked by the agent): below.

- Review fixes 2 (tests first, seen failing: 5 of the new/extended tests):
  - Critical: `on_core_error` returns a `Task`; an `Error` while `WaitingForBrowser` now also sends `Command::CancelOAuth` (the core's `Engine.oauth` stayed `Some`, so retries were refused as busy and the loopback listener stayed open).
  - Warning: `LoginFailed(Cancelled)` no longer overwrites the notice of a refused URL or of the error above. New `App.pending_cancel` is set when the app ends an OAuth attempt itself and sends `CancelOAuth` (`cancel_in_core`); exactly one `Cancelled` reply is consumed with it. A `Cancelled` with no login in flight is also ignored.
  - Warning: a late `Cancelled` after a new attempt started is consumed by `pending_cancel`, so the new attempt stays in flight. A user Cancel does not set the flag (the attempt stays in flight until the reply). `pending_cancel` is also cleared by `LoggedIn` and by any other `LoginFailed`, since a cancel reply would have arrived first (the core sends no reply when no OAuth was pending, so the flag must not linger).
  - Low: notices from core errors and `send_error` go through `text_safety::one_line_capped(.., 512)`.
## Phase 2 / Task 10 notes
- Checks: `cargo fmt --all --check`, clippy (`--workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -D warnings`), `cargo test --workspace --locked` (app: 135 lib tests, 5 bin tests, 3 doctests), `scripts/check-app-deps.sh`, `scripts/check-core-api.sh`, `cargo deny check` and the ignored run (`docker compose up -d --wait && scripts/create-test-users.sh && cargo test -p tessera-core -p tessera-app --locked -- --ignored --skip keyring`) all pass; `docker compose stop` afterwards. The keyring test was not run. No window was opened.
- TDD: the sanitiser tests (4 new) were written first and failed (invisible characters kept, fillers kept, marks not collapsed) before the code; the `room_list` pure-function tests and the state tests (query, paging, scroll reset) were written before the functions existed (compile errors for the missing items), then the code was added; one paging test expectation was wrong (a 20-row page still left the view too far from the end) and was fixed in the test; the empty-list scroll reset failed first and then passed. Not seen red: the views (`screens/room_list_pane.rs`; they only compile, rendering is on the human list), the `count_label`/`tab_label` helpers, and the `main_view` body-cap and account-label tests, whose helper was added at once.
- Query: `App` gains `query: RoomListQuery` (what the core has; the search is trimmed), `search_input` (the field as typed), `list_offset_y`, `list_viewport_h`, `load_more_at`. `Message::{TabSelected, SearchChanged, RoomListScrolled}`. A changed query sends exactly one `SetRoomListQuery`; an identical one (including a whitespace-only change to the search) sends nothing. A new query clears `load_more_at`. The query is kept across logout because the core keeps its own (engine.rs `room_list_query` watch).
- Windowing (Q12): pure `visible_range`, `spacer_heights` in `room_list.rs` (`ROW_HEIGHT` 64, `OVERSCAN_ROWS` 10). `visible_range` clamps a stale offset and treats NaN as 0. The pane builds only that window between two `space()` spacers; a test checks `top + rows + bottom == len * ROW_HEIGHT`. An empty list builds no scrollable, so the stored offset is reset to 0 when the list is empty and on logout.
- Badges (Q9): pure `badge_for(&RoomSummary) -> Badge` (`Highlight(n)` from `highlights`, `Count(n)` from `unread_notifications`, `Dot` for unread messages or marked unread, else `None`); one test per rule and per precedence. Counts show as `99+` above 99.
- Rows: avatar slot (an empty 40 px space; Task 11 fills it) (superseded in Task 11), name, "Invite" tag, preview `sender_name` or "You": body, badge. Clicking sends `SelectRoom`; the selected row has the palette's `selection` background. Empty states: "No rooms" (All, Rooms), "No direct messages", "No spaces", and "No match" with a non-blank search.
- Paging (Q8): pure `should_load_more(visible_end, len, total)` (`LOAD_MORE_MARGIN` 50); `App::check_paging` runs after every `RoomListChanged`, `RoomListLoaded` and scroll, and sends `LoadMoreRooms` at most once per loaded length (scripted scroll/append test). Note: `total_rooms` is the account total, not the filtered count, so with a filter active one `LoadMoreRooms` per loaded length may be sent near the end; harmless (a no-op in the core when nothing more is listed).
- Timing (Q17, Task 7 follow-up): `room_list_batches_under_16ms` now starts every run at a scrolled-to-the-end app with `total_rooms = Some(100_000)`, so each batch also runs the paging bookkeeping (visible range, `should_load_more`, the `LoadMoreRooms` send), and asserts that it did (`load_more_at == Some(len)`). Medians (debug, this machine, 15 runs after a warm-up): reset 2000: 1.73 ms; 4 x append 500: 0.97 ms; mixed on 2000: 0.41 ms; clear then reset 2000: 0.59 ms. Budget 16 ms. The avatar bookkeeping does not exist yet (Task 11). (superseded in Task 11)
- Sanitiser (`text_safety`, by hand, no new dependency). `one_line` now also removes U+E0000-E007F, U+206A-206F, U+180E, U+FFF9-FFFB, U+034F, U+FE00-FE0F and U+E0100-E01EF; maps U+115F, U+1160, U+3164, U+FFA0 and U+2800 to U+FFFD; and drops combining marks (U+0300-036F, U+1AB0-1AFF, U+1DC0-1DFF, U+20D0-20FF, U+FE20-FE2F) beyond `MAX_COMBINING_MARKS` = 4 in a row after one base character (the run is not reset by removed characters). Each range has a test. Side effect: emoji variation selectors (U+FE0F) are dropped, as asked.
- Untrusted text: room names go through `row_name` (`one_line_capped`, 256); the preview is `preview_line`: sender (name capped at 64, "You" for own messages, else the user ID) and body capped at 120, each sanitised. Message bodies in the right pane are capped at `MAX_BODY_CHARS` = 4096 (`display_lines`), and the status bar user ID goes through `account_label` (sanitised, capped at 256). Nothing here is logged.
- APIs used, confirmed in `~/.cargo/registry/src/index.crates.io-1949cf8c6b5b557f/` (iced_widget 0.14.2, iced_core 0.14.0; the build also compiled under clippy):
  - iced_widget-0.14.2: `Scrollable::on_scroll` scrollable.rs:164; `Viewport::absolute_offset` :1739 (`AbsoluteOffset { x, y }` iced_core widget/operation/scrollable.rs:120) and `Viewport::bounds` :1775; `space()` helpers.rs:1748, `Space::width`/`height` space.rs:44/:50; `Column::extend` column.rs:157; `Button::{height :131, padding :137, clip :177, style :184}`, `button::{primary :598, secondary :613, text :673}`, `button::Style` :487 (`background`, `text_color`); `container::Style` container.rs:462 (`text_color`, `background`, `border`); `TextInput::on_input` text_input.rs:172; `Row::align_y` row.rs:118.
  - iced_core-0.14.0: `Text::wrapping` widget/text.rs:153 and `text::Wrapping::None` text.rs:181; `border::rounded` border.rs:24, `Border::{color :54, width :70}`.
- Open issues: (1) the avatar slot is empty until Task 11 (superseded in Task 11). (2) The row height is fixed, so long previews are cut at the row edge (`Wrapping::None` plus `clip`), not with an ellipsis, beyond the 120-character cap. (3) The windowed range comes from the last reported scroll position; before the first scroll event it assumes an 800 px viewport (`DEFAULT_VIEWPORT_HEIGHT`). (4) A search is sent per keystroke (one command each, no debounce); the core sets the filter with `reset_to_one_page`.
- New human items (rule 9, not ticked by the agent): in the Phase 2 human checkpoint below.
- Open issue fixed (flaky `last_message_preview_shows_own_message`, failed in the Task 9 and Task 10 gate runs). Root cause: not a core bug and not a race with `listen_to_room`. The shared test accounts piled up rooms across runs (alice had 411 joined rooms; every run adds about 25). A core logging in as alice processes every joined room of the first sync, and matrix-sdk's event cache does that one room at a time at about 90 ms each in a debug build (visible in the `event_cache::states` lock timers: a steady ~10 room updates per second for ~40 s). The marker message's sync response is queued behind that work, so its latest-event `Set` arrived about 41 s after the room was listed, past the 30 s wait. Evidence: the test failed 4 of 6 runs of the file and 3 of 3 alone on the polluted account; with the wait raised to 150 s it passed with the preview arriving ~41 s after the send; the core's own `map_batch` cost was microseconds per batch (timed, not kept); no `Set` carried a preview before the marker because no latest event was computed until the event cache finished; read in matrix-sdk 0.19.1 `latest_events/mod.rs` (`listen_to_room`, `compute_latest_events_task`) and matrix-sdk-ui `room_list.rs` (`merge_stream_and_receiver` emits a `Set` per `RoomInfoNotableUpdate`): updates are delivered correctly, just late. No previews are lost in real use, only delayed on a first sync of a very large account in a debug build.
- Fix: new `scripts/prune-test-rooms.sh` (curl + jq; every test user leaves and forgets all joined rooms and declines all invites; prints counts only, never tokens or room names), called at the end of `scripts/create-test-users.sh`, which the gate runs before the ignored tests. It must run between test runs, not during one. No timeout, sleep or assertion was changed, so there is no Rust change and no unit test applies. After the fix: the file alone passed 5 of 5 (8/8 each, 10-18 s, with no pruning between the runs, so ~100 rooms accumulated); shellcheck (docker) clean on both scripts; full gate passes; the ignored run (`docker compose up -d --wait && scripts/create-test-users.sh && cargo test -p tessera-core -p tessera-app --locked -- --ignored --skip keyring`) passed 3 times in a row, the first after `docker compose down -v`; then `docker compose stop`. Note for a human: a release build or a smaller account is far quicker; if previews on a first sync of a 2,000+ room account feel slow, that is the SDK's event cache, not the core.

## Phase 2 / Task 11 notes
- Checks: `cargo fmt --all --check`, clippy (`--workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -D warnings`), `cargo test --workspace --locked`, `scripts/check-app-deps.sh`, `scripts/check-core-api.sh`, `cargo deny check`, and the ignored run (`docker compose up -d --wait && scripts/create-test-users.sh && cargo test -p tessera-core -p tessera-app --locked -- --ignored --skip keyring`, then `docker compose stop`): all pass (app: 166 lib tests, 5 bin tests; the ignored run: all suites ok, 0 failed).
- TDD: the `avatars` tests were written first and run against stubbed `decode_avatar` / `initials` (9 failed); the `update` tests were written before `App::avatars` existed (compile error), then with the field but no behaviour (7 failed), then the code. Mutation check: with the decoder limits removed, the limit-edge test fails.
- New `crates/app/src/avatars.rs`:
  - `decode_avatar(&[u8]) -> Option<Handle>`: avatar bytes come from other users, so they are decoded here, never passed raw to iced. Only PNG, JPEG and WebP are accepted (format guessed from the content); `Limits` are `max_image_width`/`max_image_height` = `MAX_AVATAR_SIDE` (512) and `max_alloc` = `MAX_DECODE_ALLOC` (8 MiB); the result is RGBA pixels in `Handle::from_rgba`, so iced never decodes untrusted bytes. Failure, a wrong format, a corrupt or truncated body, or a header over the limits gives `None`, which shows the initials placeholder. Tests: tiny valid PNG, 96 px, the exact limit edge (512 ok, 513 refused), JPEG, WebP, empty, garbage, truncated, damaged, GIF and SVG refused, and IHDR headers claiming 60000x60000, u32::MAX x 1 and 1 x 513 (CRC fixed up, tiny body).
  - `Avatars` (state in `App::avatars`): `requested: HashSet<AvatarId>` and `states: HashMap<AvatarId, AvatarState>` (`Ready(Handle)` / `Unavailable`). `take_unrequested` returns each not-yet-requested ID once (also within one batch) and marks it; at most `MAX_REQUEST_BATCH` (128) per command, the rest stays unrequested for a later change. `answer` ignores IDs never requested, stores `Ready` for a valid image and `Unavailable` for `None` or an image that fails validation. **Retry policy: none this session.** Every ID is requested once per session whatever the answer (in flight, answered, or answered `None`), so scrolling cannot cause a retry storm against a failing homeserver. The sets are cleared on `LoggedOut` and `SessionExpired` (`back_to_login`). Its `Debug` shows counts only (no MXC URIs).
  - `initials(name)`: takes the already-sanitised name (`room_list::row_name`), skips leading `#`, `@`, `!`, splits on whitespace and on the sanitiser's line-break mark, drops leading U+FFFD marks, and takes the first character of the first two words only if it is alphanumeric (so emoji, ZWJ sequences and flag halves never give a half glyph), uppercased with the first char of the uppercase mapping (`ß` gives `S`). Empty, blank, symbol-only or marker-only names give `"?"`, drawn on a neutral grey disc. Unit tests: plain, single word, `#room:server`, `@user:server`, `!abc:server`, accented, Cyrillic, CJK, emoji, hostile sanitised name, and a 200,000-character name.
  - `placeholder_color(room_id)`: FNV-1a of the ID into the 8-colour `PLACEHOLDER_COLORS` list (stable across Rust versions; unit-tested for stability, membership and spread).
- `App::request_avatars` runs after every `RoomListChanged`, `RoomListLoaded` and scroll (`after_list_change`, together with paging): it collects the `avatar` IDs of the built rows (`visible_range` with overscan) that were never requested and sends one `FetchAvatars`, or nothing if the set is empty. A query change comes back from the core as a list change, so it is covered. Tests: a `Reset` of 100 rooms with a 20-row viewport requests exactly rows 0..30 in one command; a repeat, a re-`Reset` and `RoomListLoaded` request nothing; scrolling requests only the new rows; no avatar sends nothing; a shared avatar is requested once; a changed avatar of a visible room is requested; scrolling back and forth five times over failed (`None`) avatars sends each ID exactly once; a `Clear` + `Reset` after `None` answers asks again for nothing; logout and expiry clear the state and a new session asks again.
- Q17 timing: `room_list_batches_under_16ms` still runs all four 2,000-room shapes through `update`, which now includes the avatar bookkeeping; each run also asserts that exactly one `FetchAvatars` was sent and that the bookkeeping is non-empty. Medians (debug, this machine, 15 runs after a warm-up): reset 2000 1.46 ms; 4 x append 500 1.39 ms; mixed on 2000 0.48 ms; clear then reset 2000 0.60 ms (budget 16 ms).
- View (`room_list_pane::avatar_view`): a 40 px round slot: the decoded image when `Ready`, otherwise the initials (white on the room's palette colour, or on the theme's muted grey for `"?"`). The placeholder stays when unavailable. A smoke test builds the element in every state.
- APIs used, confirmed in `~/.cargo/registry/src/index.crates.io-1949cf8c6b5b557f/`:
  - image-0.25.10: `ImageReader::new` src/io/image_reader_type.rs:89, `format` :110, `limits` :137, `with_guessed_format` :254, `decode` :311 (applies the dimension limits and `limits.reserve(decoder.total_bytes())` before allocating); `Limits` (`#[non_exhaustive]`, fields `max_image_width`/`max_image_height`/`max_alloc`, so built from `Limits::default()` and assigned) src/io/limits.rs:30-43; `DynamicImage::into_rgba8` src/images/dynimage.rs:418; `ImageBuffer::dimensions` src/images/buffer.rs:705, `into_raw` :695; `ImageFormat::{Png, Jpeg, WebP}` src/io/format.rs:22.
  - iced_core-0.14.0: `image::Handle::from_rgba(width, height, pixels)` src/image.rs:147; `Handle`'s `Debug` omits the pixels (:185); `Text::color` src/widget/text.rs:169; `border::rounded` src/border.rs:24; `Color::from_rgb` (const) src/color.rs:74.
  - iced_widget-0.14.2: `image()` helper src/helpers.rs:1812 (behind the `image` feature, via `image-without-codecs`), `Image::new` src/image.rs:74, `.width` :91, `.height` :97, `.border_radius` :175; `Container::{width :121, height :127, center_x :145, center_y :150}` src/container.rs.
- Open issues (Phase 2 wrap-up list):
  1. iced text-input buffers are not zeroized (Q19): the password and composer text live in iced's own buffers while typed.
  2. Decoding untrusted thumbnails relies on the `image` crate (png, zune-jpeg, image-webp). It decodes through `image::ImageReader` with `Limits` (512 px, 8 MiB allocation) from at most 1 MiB of input (the core's cap), and runs inside `update` (a 512 px image decodes in a few milliseconds; the usual 96 px thumbnail much less). A decoder bug would be reachable by any user who sets an avatar in a room you see.
  3. The CLI and the app share one data dir and keychain entry (Q20); running both at once is unsupported (SQLite store lock).
  4. A failed avatar fetch is not retried this session (no backoff, by design); it shows initials until the next login or restart. A transient homeserver outage therefore leaves initials for those rooms.
  5. Avatars of rooms are only fetched for the built rows (visible plus 10 rows of overscan); a fast scrollbar drag can show initials for a moment before the thumbnails arrive.
  6. Initials ignore emoji-led words by design (no half glyphs); a name made only of emoji shows `?`.
- Phase 2 agent work is done. The human checkpoint below stays open. New human items (rule 9, not ticked by the agent): in the Phase 2 human checkpoint below.

## Phase 2 / Task 11 wrap-up review fixes
- Local-homeserver guard (rule 6): new `scripts/require-local-homeserver.sh` (sourced by `prune-test-rooms.sh` and `create-test-users.sh` after `.env.test`, before any request) exits 1 unless `HOMESERVER_URL` is `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`, `http://localhost:<port>` or `http://[::1]:<port>` (one optional trailing `/`). Demonstration, each run from a scratch copy of `scripts/` with a `.env.test` whose `HOMESERVER_URL` was replaced; every run printed `refusing to use HOMESERVER_URL: it must be http://127.0.0.1:<port>, ...` on stderr and made no request:
  - `HOMESERVER_URL=https://matrix.org`: `prune-test-rooms.sh` exit 1, `create-test-users.sh` exit 1.
  - `http://example.com:8008`, `http://127.0.0.1.evil.com:8008`, `http://localhost:8008@evil.com`, `http://127.0.0.1` (no port), `http://localhost:8008/x`: both scripts exit 1 each.
  - Control: `http://127.0.0.1:9` (loopback, nothing listening) passes the guard and then fails on the connection (exit 7 from curl for `prune-test-rooms.sh`).
- `prune-test-rooms.sh`: the password reaches jq through its environment (`env.PRUNE_PASSWORD`), not argv; a failed leave or forget is counted, no longer skips `/logout`, is reported on stderr, and makes the script exit non-zero after all three users were handled (`prune` is deliberately not called with `||`, which would disable `set -e` inside it; found while testing the first version). `create-test-users.sh`: the script directory is captured before `cd`, so the call to `prune-test-rooms.sh` no longer depends on a relative `$0`. `shellcheck -x scripts/*.sh` (docker `koalaman/shellcheck`) reports nothing.
- Rust tests: `common::is_local_homeserver` / `assert_local_homeserver` in `crates/core/tests/common/mod.rs` (called by `TestEnv::load`, so before any credentials are read or sent) and the same pair in `crates/app/tests/bridge.rs` (`TestEnv::load` and `http_json`); a non-ignored test per crate checks accepted and refused URLs (userinfo trick, suffix host, https, missing port, path).
- `text_safety`: `is_combining_mark` now covers U+0483-0489, U+0591-05BD, U+05BF, U+05C1-05C2, U+05C4-05C5, U+05C7, U+0610-061A, U+064B-065F, U+0670, U+06D6-06DC, U+06DF-06E4, U+06E7-06E8, U+06EA-06ED, U+0E31, U+0E34-0E3A, U+0E47-0E4E and the Vedic marks. Each range was checked against Unicode 15.0 general categories (Python `unicodedata`; no `unicode-*` crate in the lock file exposes the category): all are Mn/Me. Deviation: U+1CD0-1CFF is not covered whole, because that block also holds letters (Lo), punctuation and unassigned code points; only its Mn code points are counted (1CD0-1CD2, 1CD4-1CE0, 1CE2-1CE8, 1CED, 1CF4, 1CF8-1CF9). Newly dropped as invisible: U+17B4, U+17B5, U+180B-180F, U+1D173-1D17A, U+FFFC. New `name_or_placeholder` (`(unnamed)`) is used for room names, preview sender names and timeline sender names that are empty or only space, U+00A0, U+2000-200A or U+3000 after sanitising. Tests include `"a" + ("\u{0301}"*4 + "\u{0483}").repeat(50)` keeping 4 marks.
- Emoji and joiners: U+FE0E/FE0F and U+200C/200D are kept, one selector and one joiner per base character (a selector and a joiner do not use each other's allowance, so 👁️‍🗨️ survives); runs collapse. U+FE00-FE0D and U+E0100-E01EF are still dropped. Tag characters U+E0020-E007F are kept only right after U+1F3F4 and at most 8 of them; any other character, even a dropped one, ends the sequence. Tests: ❤️, 1️⃣, 👨‍👩‍👧, a Persian ZWNJ word and a Scotland-style flag are unchanged; 500 FE0F collapse to one; tags after a letter are dropped.
- Room selection: `RoomListChanged` deselects (and drops the draft and timeline) only while the query is the default; with a search or a non-default tab the open room and its draft stay even if the filtered list lacks the room (including a `Clear`). Logout, expiry and the default query still deselect. Tests cover search, tab, default query, return to the default query, logout and expiry.
- `pending_cancel` is cleared on `NoSession`, `LoggedOut` and `SessionExpired` (`back_to_login`). Test: `a_pending_cancel_is_forgotten_when_the_session_state_changes`.
- Checks: fmt, clippy (`--all-features --locked -D warnings`), `cargo test --workspace --locked`, `check-app-deps.sh`, `check-core-api.sh`, `cargo deny check`, shellcheck, and the ignored run against the local Synapse (`create-test-users.sh` with the new guard and prune, then `-p tessera-core -p tessera-app -- --ignored --skip keyring`; all suites ok, no flaky run) all pass; the keyring test was not run; `docker compose stop` afterwards.
- Open issues left by this step:
  1. `is_combining_mark` is still a hand-written list, not the full Unicode Mn/Me table: marks of scripts not listed (Devanagari, Bengali, Tamil, Thai tone marks outside the range and so on) still count as base characters and reset the counter, so a run alternating a listed and an unlisted mark is not capped. Closing this needs the full table (a generated range table or a crate such as `unicode-normalization`/`unicode-segmentation`, which needs approval as an app dependency). Output length is still bounded by the 256/4096 character caps.
  2. Selection keeps a hidden room only while a non-default query is active; a stale list update for the old query arriving just after the user returned to the default query can still drop the open room (the following `Reset` shows it again, but the draft is gone).
  3. The subdivision-flag rule keeps up to 8 invisible tag characters after any U+1F3F4, a small amount of smuggling room for a hostile sender, not checked against the valid flag tag list.
  4. A selector and a joiner each survive once per base, so a hostile string can carry two invisible characters per visible character within the length caps.

## Phase 2 open issues (summary)
- A DM seen before its `m.direct` account data stays non-DM and shows under the wrong tab (Task 4 notes).
- An OAuth flow can be left pending in the core in narrow races, refusing logins for up to 10 minutes (Task 9 notes).
- A delayed `CancelOAuth` may cancel a newer attempt (Task 9 notes).
- `text_safety` still uses hand-written ranges (Task 10 and Task 11 wrap-up notes): unlisted combining marks (Indic, Syriac, Tibetan, supplementary planes) bypass the cap; joiners and selectors after any base allow look-alike names; up to 8 tag characters survive after U+1F3F4; invisible-only names escape "(unnamed)".
- Avatar decoding runs on the UI thread without `catch_unwind` or cache eviction (Task 11 notes).
- Avatar fetches are thumbnail-only (Q11 deviation; Task 5 notes).
- The first sync of an account with hundreds of rooms takes about 40 s in debug because of SDK per-room work (Task 10 notes).
- No search debounce (Task 10 notes).
- `scripts/create-test-users.sh` puts the password in curl argv (local test homeserver only).
- The three copies of the loopback check (two Rust test helpers and `scripts/require-local-homeserver.sh`) must be kept in sync (Task 11 wrap-up notes).

## Phase 4 / Task 9 notes (log capture and data at rest, core side)
- Step done: `crates/core/tests/log_hygiene.rs` (own test binary, one ignored test, verbatim ignore reason), Q16 (c) unit tests in `paths.rs` and `session.rs`. `EncryptedPair` in `tests/common/mod.rs` now exposes the two cores' secret stores (`secrets`).
- What it checks: a global `tracing_subscriber` registry with two layers on one flow, `info` and `DEFAULT_FILTER`, each into its own in-memory buffer. The flow: alice and carol reset to B0, encrypted room, each core looks the peer up, sentinel bodies both ways, identity reset on alice's core 1, recovery enabled (key captured), alice's second core logs in, SAS between the two alice cores, `Recover` with the key on core 2, device rename, then the scans, then logout of all three cores and the log scans. 12 sentinel values: access token and store passphrase of the 3 stored records (no refresh token is present with password login), 2 passwords, the recovery key (with and without spaces), 2 bodies. UTF-8 and JSON-escaped forms are searched. Files under the 3 data roots (39 files, about 8 MB, -wal/-shm included) are scanned while the sessions are live; the logs after logout.
- Result: no sentinel in the logs (both filters) and none on disk. The test prints only counts, kinds and file names.
- Self-check: first, a sentinel logged with `tracing::info!` into a separate discarded buffer and one written to a scratch file (`nested/file.db-wal`) must be found, an unrelated sentinel must not. Seen red: with the `info!` line removed the self-check failed ("the capture or the scanner missed the self-check sentinel").
- Order differs slightly from Q16 (a): SAS first, then `Recover` on core 2 (a recovered device is already verified, so a self-verification request after it would test a different path). Bodies are sent before the identity reset.
- Flake found and fixed in the test (not the core): sending right after the reset Security event sometimes left alice's own message unsent for 5 to 60 s (3 of 14 runs; the first run after `create-test-users.sh` failed twice at the 30 s pump timeout), and 8/8 runs passed with a 5 s pause there. The cause is the restore window of `SyncPause` (sync restarting, send queue off) right after the event; the test now sends the bodies before the reset and retries `EnableRecovery` on `Busy`. Not fixed in the core: a message sent in the seconds after `ResetIdentity` completes can be delayed (Task 10/12 should not assume it goes out at once). After the change 10/10 runs passed, plus the two official runs below.
- Ignored runs right after `scripts/create-test-users.sh`: first 19.33 s, repeat 18.86 s, both green (earlier runs 16 to 22 s).
- Unit tests (Q16 c): `paths.rs` lists the data root after `create_session_store_dir` + `write_last_account` (only `last_account` and `sessions`, and `sessions/` holds only the store dir); `session.rs` checks that oversize/error strings carry no sentinel and that `Serialize` and `serde_json::to_*` appear in no other source file. Both were written after the code and passed at once (they pin existing behaviour).
- Gate: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` all pass.
- Residual (for the Task 14 review): SDK `debug`/`trace` are not asserted; table layout and sizes of the SQLite store stay visible; the scan covers values the test knows, not derived forms (base64, URL-encoded).
- Nothing new for the human checkpoint.

### Phase 4 / Task 9 follow-up: send promptly after an identity reset
- Symptom: a message sent right after the reset's `Security` event stayed unsent for 5 to 60 s (seen in `log_hygiene.rs` before 443f88d).
- Cause (core): the status forwarder sent `Security` while the identity task still held the `SyncPause` (the status turns complete before the task ends). A send then ran with the sync stopped; the SDK waits up to 5 s for a pending own-user `/keys/query` (`matrix-sdk-crypto-0.19.1` `store/mod.rs:201-250`) that only the sync sends. The send queue was NOT the cause (it was already on).
- Fix: `IdentityOp::paused` (held by `bootstrap`/`reset` for the whole pause) is waited for by `forward_status` before it reads and sends a status; `IdentityOp::announce` makes the status after such an operation go out even when equal to the last one (a second reset in a row ends in the same status; `a_reset_right_after_login_keeps_the_new_private_keys` caught this). `PauseTarget::settled` makes the restore wait (10 s cap) until the room list is `Running` again. Task 4/5 race protection unchanged (stop before the keys exist, restart after).
- Test: `a_message_sent_right_after_a_reset_is_sent_promptly` in `e2ee_identity.rs` (ignored, `e2ee_serial`): the own "Sent" item is in the list within 10 s of the Security event. Before the fix it stalled 5 s or timed out.
- Ignored runs right after `scripts/create-test-users.sh`, first and repeat: `log_hygiene`, `e2ee_identity` (7), `e2ee_recovery` (7) all green. `log_hygiene.rs` order unchanged.
- Retraction: an earlier reading blamed an SDK timeline race (a stale local echo at "Sending" next to its "Sent" item, about 1 run in 6). It was a bug in the new test: `reset_identity_on` drops the open room's `TimelineDiff` events, so the test's item list drifted from the core's. A debug mirror of the SDK's diffs inside the core showed the SDK list was correct (`Re` x N, then `L-N`, `LeS`, `Re`); the core's mapping is faithful, so no core change is needed for the timeline. The test now runs the reset through a pump (as `log_hygiene.rs` does) and asserts exactly one own item for the message, and that it is "Sent", within 10 s: 30 of 30 runs green with SDK trace logging on (which had made the drift show up). A send right after login was not tested separately.

## Phase 4 / Task 10 notes (security state, banners, screens in the app)
- Step done: new `crates/app/src/security.rs` (pure `SecurityModel`, `banner`, `needs_identity_screen`, `can_finish_setup`, `should_clear_clipboard`, `SecurityMessage`, and the `App` handlers) and `crates/app/src/screens/security.rs` (banner slot, key, restore, identity, reset-warning and password screens). `state.rs` gains `Message::Security`, `App.security` and routes the Phase 4 security events; `main_view` is now `[banner slot, panes, status bar]` inside a fixed `stack` layer (also over the loading screen).
- Choices: the restore field keeps its text after a failed restore (so it can be corrected) and is emptied on success, logout and window close; the copied key is kept in `copied` (zeroizing, redacted) until the 60 s timer has compared the clipboard, so **Done** does not stop the clearing; a closed window cannot clear the clipboard (no task runs after exit). The account-management URL opens only if it is `https` and passes `check_browser_url`, and only while a reset is running; otherwise the reset is cancelled. A reset that fails or is cancelled always says the key backup is gone (Task 4/5 notes). While a reset runs the screen says sync is paused and it may take two minutes; silence is not read as "unchanged". `Security` ends a running reset (the core holds it until sync and the send queue are back). **Use another device** only sends `RequestSelfVerification` (the dialog is Task 11).
- Tests (unit tests through `update` with an `mpsc` sink; the pure functions table-tested; written first, seen failing to compile): banner/identity-screen tables, Done refused until ticked, one command per action, restore trimmed and kept on failure, backup-exists opens restore, reset warning then password then resend, failed/cancelled reset text, https-only approval URL, logout and close drop secrets, sentinel keys never in `Debug`. Tag test `main_view.rs::the_panes_keep_their_place_when_the_banner_and_security_screens_come_and_go`. Ignored bridge test `carol_resets_her_identity_sets_up_recovery_and_logs_out` (bridge.rs, carol).
- APIs confirmed in `~/.cargo/registry/src/*/`: iced_runtime-0.14.0 `clipboard.rs` (`read :29`, `write :49`), iced_widget-0.14.2 `checkbox.rs` (`new :119`, `label :133`, `on_toggle :155`), `stack.rs` (`update :231`, events go top layer first), `helpers.rs` (`opaque :577`), `container.rs` (`danger`, `bordered_box`), `button.rs` (`danger :658`), iced_core `font.rs:27` (`Font::MONOSPACE`), runtime `widget/operation.rs` (`focus`, already used by the composer).
- Review fix: `drop_secrets` (logout, close, disconnect, back to login, a new login) reads the clipboard at once and clears it if it still holds the copied key (`ClipboardFlushed`), so the 60 s timer is not the only path. Tests first in `state.rs`.
- Not covered: `Change key` has no screen (not in the Task 10 list); `ReauthRequired(DeleteDevices)` is ignored until Task 13.
- A first bridge run failed (my bug, not a flake): a `Security` status already in flight ended the reset wait early. Fixed test-first (`a_status_from_before_the_reset_does_not_end_it`): a running reset ends only on a status with the device verified and recovery disabled (`reset_finished`), or on a failure event.
- Checks: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (app lib 461), `scripts/check-app-deps.sh`, `scripts/check-core-api.sh` all pass. Ignored: `cargo test -p tessera-app --test bridge -- --ignored` (3 tests, about 5 s) right after `scripts/create-test-users.sh`, then repeated: both green.
- Fix round (phase4.10): on close, exit now waits until the clipboard read has been answered and the clear write issued (`flushes_pending`, `core_closed`, `App::exit_if_flushed`); `ShutdownDeadline` still exits at once. The restore field is emptied on **Back** and whenever `on_status` closes the Restore screen. Tests first (both failed before the code).

## Phase 6 / Task 1 notes
- Checks run (all pass): `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -- -D warnings`; `cargo test --workspace --locked` (new `tests/livekit_link.rs`, 2 tests); `scripts/check-app-deps.sh`; `scripts/check-app-deps.sh livekit`; `scripts/check-core-api.sh`; `scripts/check-no-webrtc-download.sh`; `cargo deny check` (cargo-deny 0.20.2, reinstalled with `--locked`; advisories, bans, licenses, sources ok, deny.toml unchanged). Ignored run `docker compose up -d --wait && scripts/create-test-users.sh && cargo test -p tessera-core --locked -- --ignored --skip keyring`: 11/11 (first recorded as 12/12, a miscount: cli 1, login 4, oauth 1, room_list 2, timeline 3), then `docker compose stop`. shellcheck (docker koalaman/shellcheck:stable) clean on both new scripts; actionlint 1.7.7 (docker) clean on ci.yml.
- Fetch script checks: fresh download and verification of `webrtc-linux-x64-release.zip` (148.9 MB), cached re-run in under 10 ms. Negative checks: a tampered pinned digest gives exit 1 and leaves nothing unpacked in the scratch cache; an unknown triple (`linux-x86`) gives exit 1; a malformed digest gives exit 1. Each was a temporary edit, reverted with `git diff` clean.
- **Finding (rule 2):** webrtc-sys 0.3.47 downloads libwebrtc whenever the `LK_CUSTOM_WEBRTC` directory does not exist, not only when the variable is unset (webrtc-sys-0.3.47/build.rs:108-113 calls `webrtc_sys_build::download_webrtc()`, which fetches `download_url()` with `reqwest` and no checksum, webrtc-sys-build-0.3.19/src/lib.rs:199-240). This was seen happening: a first `.cargo/config.toml` pointing at a missing `.libwebrtc` made webrtc-sys download into it. That unverified copy was deleted (with the `scratch-*/out/livekit_webrtc` lock dir) and webrtc-sys rebuilt from the verified copy. Fix: `LK_CUSTOM_WEBRTC` points at the committed `dev/libwebrtc/`, which always exists, and the fetch script links the verified entries into it. Checked: with no links and no env var, `cargo clean -p webrtc-sys && cargo build` fails with "Could not open ninja file: …/dev/libwebrtc/webrtc.ninja" and no download dir appears; after the fetch it builds.
- **pw_* stub rename (added 2026-10-02 after Task 8, human decision for native PipeWire):** libwebrtc.a's `pipewire_stubs.o` defines 37 weak `pw_*` functions that jump through pointers only its own dlopen loader (`modules_portal::InitializeStubs`) fills; 4 objects (`pipewire_session.o`, `pipewire_utils.o`, `shared_screencast_stream.o`, `video_capture_pipewire.o`) call them, 51 references in all. Linked next to libpipewire, the weak definitions bind cpal's calls and `pw_init` jumps to address 0. The fetch script renames all of them to `tessera_webrtc_pw_*` (`objcopy --redefine-syms`, map built from `nm`), so libwebrtc's capture keeps its own shims (they still dlsym the real names at run time) and the `pw_*` names resolve to libpipewire. Checks: global `pw_*` count after renaming is 0 on both `linux-x64` and `linux-arm64` (fresh download into a scratch cache), 37 W and 51 U renamed symbols each; the AV1 assembly's 572 local `pw_*` constants ("packed words", lowercase nm types) are left alone and cannot clash. An already-verified cache gets the rename on the next run (marker `.pw-renamed`); the rename is idempotent. No pinned digest of the patched archive: its bytes depend on the objcopy version. shellcheck clean.
- `scripts/check-no-webrtc-download.sh` self-check: exit 0 on the clean tree, exit 1 with a planted `target/debug/build/scratch-test0/out/livekit_webrtc`, exit 2 with a missing target dir.
- `scripts/check-app-deps.sh livekit` self-check: with a temporary `livekit` dependency in crates/app/Cargo.toml it exits 1; reverted, it exits 0 (Cargo.lock: tessera-app deps still `iced`, `tessera-core`).
- APIs used (registry src/): `livekit::webrtc` re-exports `libwebrtc::*` (livekit-0.9.3/src/lib.rs:22-23); `libwebrtc::native::{apm, create_random_uuid}` (libwebrtc-0.3.50/src/lib.rs:70-76); `AudioProcessingModule::{new, process_stream, process_reverse_stream, set_stream_delay_ms}` (native/apm.rs:25, :41, :75, :109). Linux build needs clang++: webrtc-sys-0.3.47/build.rs:531-547 (Chromium libc++ `trivial_abi`; GCC breaks the `unique_ptr` return convention) and glib/gobject/gio headers (:185-188). The libwebrtc tag `WEBRTC_TAG = "webrtc-89d790b"` is at webrtc-sys-build-0.3.19/src/lib.rs:31. Download URL: :101-106. The libwebrtc layout check uses `<triple>-release/webrtc.ninja`, matching `prebuilt_dir()` (:91-99).
- Dependency tree: `Cargo.lock` adds 75 packages and removes none; no existing package version changed. Added second versions next to existing ones: base64 0.21.7, reqwest 0.12.28 (webrtc-sys-build build-dep, rustls native roots), rand 0.9.5, rand_chacha 0.9.0, jni 0.21.1, codespan-reporting 0.13.1, objc2-* 0.3.2, windows-sys 0.45.0 / windows-targets 0.42.2. `cargo tree -i openssl-sys` and `-i native-tls` match nothing.
- First CI run (36979250879, workflow_dispatch): every Linux job and macOS failed in webrtc-sys build.rs:598, "clang++ is clang 18, but the hermetic libc++ shipped with this libwebrtc requires clang 21 or later". Ubuntu 24.04 runners have clang 16–18; the local machine has clang 22.1.8, so this didn't show locally. Fix: Linux jobs run on `ubuntu-26.04` / `ubuntu-26.04-arm` (runner-images lists clang 20.1.8, 21.1.8, 22.1.2) with `CC=clang-22 CXX=clang++-22`; `clang` dropped from the apt line (preinstalled). `.github/actionlint.yaml` declares the two labels, because actionlint 1.7.12 (latest; 1.7.7 too) doesn't know them yet. actionlint 1.7.12 clean.
- Same run, Windows: link failed with `LNK2038 RuntimeLibrary 'MT_StaticRelease' doesn't match 'MD_DynamicRelease'` (libwebrtc uses the static CRT). macOS: `livekit_link` aborted at startup, `+[NSString stringForStdString:]: unrecognized selector` (ObjC categories in the static lib not loaded). Fix: `.cargo/config.toml` per-target flags taken from livekit/rust-sdks' own `.cargo/config.toml` (main): `+crt-static` for `{x86_64,aarch64}-pc-windows-msvc`, `-C link-args=-ObjC` (rustflags and rustdocflags) for `{x86_64,aarch64}-apple-darwin`, and `-C link-arg=-fuse-ld=lld` for `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu` (upstream: GNU ld fails on libwebrtc.a); the CI apt line gains `lld`. The livekit README documents the `-ObjC` requirement ("### MacOS"). The aarch64 and Windows flags can only be checked by CI.
- Open issues: CI on Windows, macOS and `ubuntu-26.04-arm` is unverified until the PR runs (Windows link libs, macOS frameworks, the arm64 zip). Build cost: the core test binary links libwebrtc (static); watch runner disk and time. ## Phase 6 / Task 2 notes
- Checks run (all pass): `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -- -D warnings`; `cargo test --workspace --locked` (123 passed, 15 ignored); `scripts/check-app-deps.sh` (and `livekit`); `scripts/check-core-api.sh`; `scripts/check-no-webrtc-download.sh`; `cargo deny check`. From a fresh volume (`docker compose down -v && docker compose up -d --wait`: synapse, livekit, lk-jwt all healthy; `scripts/create-test-users.sh`), the full ignored suite `cargo test -p tessera-core --locked -- --ignored --skip keyring` passed 3 times in a row, 14/14 (11 existing plus 3 in `tests/rtc_stack.rs`); `rtc_stack` alone passed 6/6 after the rate-limit fix. `tests/rtc_stack.rs`: 3 ignore reasons = 3 `#[tokio::test]`.
- Manual smoke check before the tests (statuses, URLs and lengths only, no token printed): `/rtc/transports` gives `[{"type":"livekit","livekit_service_url":"http://127.0.0.1:8080"}]`; OpenID token → `POST /get_token` gives `url: ws://127.0.0.1:7880` and an 807-character JWT.
- **Rate-limit finding (not a flake):** in the 3rd of the first runs, `delayed_state_event_is_sent_by_the_server` timed out. The Synapse log showed `Failed to send delayed event … LimitExceededError: 429` from `create_and_send_nonmember_event` (`rc_message` default 0.2/s, burst 10, used up by the tests' room creations). Synapse drops such a delayed event and does not retry (synapse/handlers/delayed_events.py:494-498). Dev fix: `rc_message` and `rc_delayed_event_mgmt` relaxed in `dev/synapse/start.sh` (after the rebase onto Phase 2, `rc_message` comes from main's `# tessera-dev-rate-limits` block and this task keeps only `rc_delayed_event_mgmt`, so the key is not set twice). Consequence for Task 7: on a real server, a crash-safety leave event can be lost to rate limiting, so membership expiry must not rely on it alone (the member event's own expiry handling stays).
- **Design findings (rule 2/8, resolved without a new MSC choice):** Synapse 1.151 has no MSC4502/MSC4512 support (no `proxy_prefix`/`msc4502` in the image), so lk-jwt-service runs in its deprecated-but-supported standalone mode. It validates OpenID tokens at `https://<server_name>:8448` (lk-jwt-service v0.7.0 src/helper.rs:1192-1247; explicit-port server names skip discovery), hence the dev-only TLS listener and the shared network namespace. livekit and lk-jwt see `localhost` as the synapse container.
- lk-jwt-service v0.7.0 facts used later (source at tag v0.7.0, read in the scratchpad): routes `/get_token`, `/sfu/get` (legacy), `/delegate_delayed_leave`, `/sfu_webhook`, `/healthz` (src/handler.rs:1271-1278); `SfuRequest { room_id, slot_id, openid_token, member { id, claimed_user_id, claimed_device_id }, delay_id, delay_timeout, delay_cs_api_url }` (src/requests.rs:71-91); the response `{ url, jwt }` (:99); `claimed_user_id` must equal the OpenID subject (seen: a mangled ID got `M_UNAUTHORIZED`); identity and room alias hashes (src/helper.rs:198-222); `LIVEKIT_URL` is both the URL returned to clients and the API URL (src/config.rs:223, handler.rs:896).
- SDK and ruma APIs used: `Client::discover_rtc_transports` (matrix-sdk-0.19.1/src/client/mod.rs:2898); `RtcTransport::LiveKit(LiveKitRtcTransport { service_url })` (ruma-client-api-0.25.0/src/rtc.rs:120); `account::request_openid_token::v3::Request::new(user_id)` and its `Response { access_token, token_type, matrix_server_name, expires_in }` (account/request_openid_token.rs:39-58); `delayed_events::delayed_state_event::unstable::Request::new_raw` (delayed_state_event.rs:101) with `DelayParameters::Timeout { timeout }` (delayed_events.rs:143, query `org.matrix.msc4140.delay`); `state::get_state_event_for_key::v3::Request::new` (get_state_event_for_key.rs:52); `matrix_sdk::reqwest` re-export (lib.rs:33). livekit: `Room::connect` (room/mod.rs:567), `Room::local_participant`, `Room::close`.
- Images: `livekit/livekit-server:v1.13.7` (has /bin/sh and wget, used for the healthcheck); `ghcr.io/element-hq/lk-jwt-service:0.7.0` (no shell; built-in `/lk-jwt-service-healthcheck`, overridden to 2 s). The lk-jwt image is amd64 here.
- Secrets hygiene: the LiveKit key and secret are test-only values in `.env.test`, like the test passwords. Nothing in the tests prints a token, OpenID token or JWT. While reading the generated Synapse config, its throwaway `registration_shared_secret`/`macaroon_secret_key` lines were printed to the agent's own terminal output; the volume was then recreated (`down -v`), so those values no longer exist.
- Open issues: the CI `integration` job pulls two more images (time to be checked on the PR run). UDP through docker's userland proxy works locally; CI is unverified until the run. lk-jwt logs a health-check line every 2 s (noisy compose logs only).
- New human items: none beyond the checkpoint list (Element Call interop is already there). Run `docker compose down -v` once locally to pick up the new Synapse config.

## Phase 6 / Task 3 notes
- Checks run (all pass): `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -- -D warnings`; `cargo test --workspace --locked` (141 passed, 15 ignored; new: 5 `api::call` unit tests, 5 `call_settings` unit tests, 2 `paths` tests, 1 doctest, 5 tests in `tests/call_settings.rs`, which need no server and so are not ignored); `scripts/check-app-deps.sh` (and `livekit`); `scripts/check-core-api.sh`; `scripts/check-no-webrtc-download.sh`; `cargo deny check`; ignored suite `cargo test -p tessera-core --locked -- --ignored --skip keyring` against the compose stack: 14/14. shellcheck clean on `scripts/check-core-api.sh`.
- API (rule 7, SDK-free; `api/call.rs`, commands and events in `api/mod.rs`): see the plan's "API additions". One deliberate change from the draft plan: settings are one `UpdateAudioSettings(AudioSettings)` command (validated, saved, echoed as `CoreEvent::AudioSettings`) instead of a setter per field. Per-user volume, mute, deafen, push-to-talk and the mic test stay separate commands because they change often or are momentary. The string-newtype macro is shared between `types.rs` and `call.rs` and now derives transparent serde, so `RoomId`, `UserId` and `DeviceId` gained `Serialize`/`Deserialize` (additive).
- Engine: `GetAudioSettings`, `UpdateAudioSettings` and `SetUserVolume` work now (load on first use, file I/O through `spawn_blocking`). The call and audio commands answer honestly until their tasks land: `JoinCall`/`DeclineCall` give `NotLoggedIn` or `Call("calls are not available yet")`; `LeaveCall`, `SetMicMuted` and `SetDeafened` give `NotInCall`; `ListAudioDevices` and `StartMicTest` give `Audio("audio devices are not available yet")`; `PushToTalk` and `StopMicTest` are no-ops. tessera-cli ignores the new events until Task 12; the iced app (Phase 2, on main) ignores them in an explicit match arm until the Phase 6 UI tasks.
- `call_settings.json`: `{ "version": 1, "settings": AudioSettings }`, written atomically (temp file, `sync_all`, rename) with mode 0o600 under the 0o700 root. A corrupt or unknown-version file gives the defaults plus one `Error(Storage(..))` with a fixed message (serde text is never forwarded, since it can quote file content); the file is kept until the next save. Values loaded from disk are validated like commands. `100` % user volumes are not stored.
- `scripts/check-core-api.sh` also rejects the media crates (`livekit`, `libwebrtc`, `webrtc_sys`/`webrtc-sys`, `cpal`, `sonora`, `nnnoiseless`, `tract`/`tract_onnx`/`tract_core`, as whole words) in `api/`, the public modules and lib.rs re-exports. Negative self-checks (temporary line in `api/call.rs`, reverted): `use livekit::Room;`, `use cpal::Device;`, `use sonora::AudioProcessing;`, `use tract_onnx::prelude::*;` and `use webrtc_sys::apm;` all exit 1; a comment with "contract, extracted" exits 0; a multi-line `pub use livekit::{ Room, };` in lib.rs exits 1; reverted, it exits 0.
- TDD note: the tests were written together with the code and run green; they were not run red first.
- Open issues: none.
- New human item: approve the call API additions as the UI-phase contract (already on the checkpoint list).

PipeWire IDs are the node name, src/host/pipewire/device.rs:214), `SupportedStreamConfig::buffer_size` and `SupportedBufferSize::Range` (src/lib.rs:460, :693) with `BufferSize::Fixed` becoming `node.latency` (src/host/pipewire/device.rs:178-183), sinks enumerated as `Duplex` (device.rs:910-922), `Host::id` and `HostId::PipeWire` (generated by `impl_platform_host!`, src/platform/mod.rs:154, :288, :825), `SampleRate = u32`, `StreamConfig` (Copy; src/lib.rs:223, :451), `SupportedStreamConfigRange::{min_sample_rate, max_sample_rate, with_sample_rate}` (:685-708), `Error::kind` / `ErrorKind::{PermissionDenied, DeviceNotAvailable, DeviceBusy, UnsupportedConfig}` (src/error.rs:10-45, :161), `Sample`/`FromSample`/`SizedSample` re-exports (src/lib.rs:194). rtrb `RingBuffer::new`, `Producer::{push, slots, push_entire_slice, buffer}`, `Consumer::{pop, slots, pop_entire_slice}` (src/lib.rs:145-746, src/chunks.rs:307, :522). rubato `Async::new_sinc(ratio, max_relative, &SincInterpolationParameters, chunk, channels, FixedAsync)` (src/asynchro.rs:297), `SincInterpolationParameters::new(len, WindowFunction)` (asynchro_sinc.rs), `Resampler::{process_into_buffer, input_frames_next, input_frames_max, output_frames_next, output_frames_max}` (src/lib.rs:263-478), `audioadapter_buffers::direct::InterleavedSlice::{new, new_mut}` (audioadapter-buffers-5.2.0/src/direct.rs:1091-1115).
- **Crash found in Task 8, fixed in Task 1:** in one binary with libwebrtc, opening a PipeWire stream crashed (SIGSEGV; gdb: `cpal::host::pipewire::stream::PwInitGuard::new` calls address 0), because libwebrtc's weak `pw_*` stubs bound cpal's calls. The fetch script now renames them (Task 1 notes). A PulseAudio-protocol backend (cpal `pulseaudio`) was a stopgap for a few hours and was dropped (human decision 2026-10-02: native PipeWire, for latency and the Steam Frame). Proof: the ignored, output-only `livekit_link::hardware_audio_output_opens_in_a_binary_linking_libwebrtc` (named `hardware_` so CI and the agent gate skip it; the agent runs it explicitly, output only) passes, and the formerly crashing `tests/call_settings.rs` passes; mutation: linked against an archive with the original names restored, the same test dies with SIGSEGV. `nm` on the test binary shows `U pw_init`, resolved from `libpipewire-0.3.so.0` (`ldd`).
- Added with the fix (2 unit tests; the totals recorded for Tasks 4 to 7 predate them): 10 ms device buffers (`ten_ms_buffer`: cpal `BufferSize::Fixed` within the device's range, instead of the host default) and, on PipeWire, inputs without sinks (`without_sinks`: cpal offers every sink as an input too, capturing what plays on it). Known limitation until Task 14: cpal's PipeWire backend also lists other applications' streams (`Stream/Output/Audio`, `Stream/Input/Audio`) as devices, and its public API cannot tell them from real ones.
- Design notes: cpal has no hot-plug notifications, so the watcher polls `list_devices` every 2 s (a failed poll keeps the last list). Underruns are counted in samples, overruns in samples, playback drops in frames (`IoStats`). The resampler's ratio is fixed for now; `MAX_RATIO_RELATIVE` 1.1 leaves room for drift-tracking later. The watcher thread sends `AudioDevices` through `blocking_send` on the engine's event channel (it is a plain thread, not a runtime one).
- Open issues: real devices (hot-plug, Bluetooth, 44.1 kHz USB devices, macOS permission prompt) are human items. The capture/playback clock-drift handling is a frame drop at 60 ms; the audible glitch is a 10 ms skip (fine for voice, to be checked by ear). Windows and macOS device code is CI-compiled only.
- New human items: none beyond the checkpoint list (device hot-plug and the macOS microphone prompt are already there).

- Checks run (all pass): `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -- -D warnings` (a first run failed on a `collapsible_if` in gate.rs, fixed); `cargo test --workspace --locked` (tessera-audio 29 passed, 2 ignored; workspace total 171 passed, 17 ignored); `scripts/check-app-deps.sh` (and `livekit`); `scripts/check-core-api.sh`; `scripts/check-no-webrtc-download.sh`; `cargo deny check` (sonora's 7 crates and nnnoiseless: BSD-3-Clause, deny.toml unchanged); ignored suite 14/14.
- Measured (debug test build with the DSP crates at opt-level 3, x86_64): AEC ERLE 36.9 dB after 4 s on the synthetic echo path; RNNoise -15.5 dB and WebRTC NS (High) -20.7 dB on stationary white noise at -26 dBFS. Mutation checks: with AEC disabled the AEC test measures 0.0 dB and fails; with RNNoise bypassed the NS test measures 0.0 dB and fails. Both reverted.
- **sonora #34 reproduced, and the guard confirmed** (`cargo test --release -p tessera-audio -- --ignored timing`, x86_64): after 3 s of far-end speech, a silent far end costs 482.7 µs mean / 613.6 µs worst per 10 ms frame without the denormal guard, and 88.2 µs / 120.0 µs with it (full default chain: HPF, AEC3, RNNoise, AGC2). Only silence that follows speech shows it: silence from the start was 96 µs vs 91 µs, because the filter states have to decay into denormals first.

- `unsafe`: `crates/audio/src/denormals.rs` (decision 7), with SAFETY comments and a test that denormals flush on the calling thread and stay IEEE on others; since Task 13b the CUDA loop's calls into the CUDA runtime (`crates/audio/src/onnx/stateful_loop.rs`, feature `onnxruntime`), and since its part 4 one call of ONNX Runtime's `CopyTensors` for the WebGPU loop (same file); since Task 15 the x86-64-v3 CPU check (`crates/core/src/cpu_level.rs`, assembly) and the processing thread made through PipeWire's real-time module (`crates/audio/src/pw/thread.rs`: its thread-utils calls, the exported `pw_thread_utils_create`/`join` and `pthread_self`, declared there because the bindings lack them); since Task 15 part 4 the calls into libwebrtc's Opus in the `opus_profile` example (`crates/core/examples/opus_profile.rs`, a measuring tool, not shipped). - Tests: `voice` module (8): mixing with volumes, removal and clamping; underrun silence plus count; trim of a source far ahead; deafen; mute (silence sent, meter live); loopback; `the_echo_reference_is_what_is_played` (ERLE ≥ 20 dB with source volume 70 % and master 80 %; mutation: rendering `SILENCE` instead of the playback frame gives 2.9 dB and fails; reverted). Core `audio_session` (3): settings mapping, DeepFilter → RNNoise fallback, device changes need a restart.

- Design notes: the processing thread never takes a lock; `VoiceControl` sits behind a `std::sync::Mutex` only between the engine and the reporting task. Adding a source allocates (on the control side; the `Vec` on the processing thread has capacity 16). Outgoing frames are always produced (silence when gated) so the sender's timing never depends on the gate; Opus DTX (Task 8) makes silence cheap. In a mic test the outgoing stream is drained and discarded by the reporting task.
- Open issues: the mic test and every real device path are human items. The `VoiceControl` command queue (64) returns `false` when full (a stuck processing thread); callers ignore that for now.

  - `/sfu/get` body `{room, openid_token, device_id, delay_*?}` (openIDSFU.ts:189-224).
