- Test findings: the first two-device run failed with "could not publish the call membership". Cause: carol's power level (0) was below `state_default` (50) in a room created by a plain client. Element sets `org.matrix.msc3401.call.member: 0` in rooms it creates. The test now does the same through `m.room.power_levels`, and the core maps `M_FORBIDDEN` to "you are not allowed to join calls in this room". Mutation: without the restart task, the membership was gone after 25 s and the test failed; reverted.
- Test helpers: `login_and_run` and `wait_for_room` moved from tests/timeline.rs to tests/common (shared, no behaviour change).
- Open issues:
  - (1) `JoinCall` runs in the engine loop, so other commands wait for the join's network round trips (as login does).
  - (2) Per Task 2, Synapse drops a delayed leave that hits `rc_message`. On a real server the membership's `expires` (4 h) is the last fallback, and other clients also treat a member whose SFU participant is gone as left (Task 8).
  - (3) Participant `muted`/`speaking` are placeholders until media.
  - (4) `DeclineCall` is a stub until Task 10.

Cause: cpal's PipeWire backend bound to libwebrtc's weak `pw_*` stubs once the core linked both. The fix (the fetch script renames the stubs; native PipeWire stays, with 10 ms buffers) is folded into the Task 1 and Task 4 commits; see their notes.

It can be re-added if the human checkpoint finds sonora's AEC lacking. New **Task 8b, multi-SFU**: Element Call's default mode publishes each member to their own homeserver's SFU, so without 8b, members from other homeservers are not heard.
- Open issues: (1) device changes during a call apply to the next call; (2) `JoinCall` (now including the SFU connection) blocks the engine loop for its duration; (3) the bitrate is applied at publish only; (4) no media E2EE yet (Task 9); in an encrypted room, Element Call members expect E2EE media.

Task 3: the iced app's exhaustive `CoreEvent` match gets an explicit arm ignoring the call and audio events until the UI tasks. Task 8: the app's `CoreConfig` literals gain `audio` (`AudioBackend::System` in `main.rs`, the default in `tests/bridge.rs`). Task 2: `rc_message` is set once (main's `# tessera-dev-rate-limits` block). CI: the integration run is `-p tessera-core -p tessera-app ... --skip keyring --skip hardware_`. `Cargo.lock` was rebuilt per commit from the rebased parent (`cargo metadata --offline`): all 114 branch-only lock entries keep their versions and none of main's changed. Seen during the rebase: a stale cargo fingerprint (an IDE checking the same target/ concurrently is the likely cause) made one quick check pass wrongly, hence the separate per-commit run.

- Element Call 0.26.1 / matrix-js-sdk v43 behaviour followed (read at those tags in the scratchpad): transport per membership `CallMembership.getTransport` (js-sdk src/matrixrtc/CallMembership.ts:419-440; `multi_sfu` → own `foci_preferred[0]`, `oldest_membership` → the oldest membership's, EC src/state/SessionBehaviors.ts:53); LiveKit identity of a session membership `${sender}:${device_id}` (`rtcBackendIdentity`, CallMembership.ts:106-116; a sha256 identity only for `m.rtc.member`); one connection per distinct `livekit_service_url` (EC remoteMembers/ConnectionManager.ts:113, :260); a participant counts only if its identity matches a membership on that transport (remoteMembers/MatrixLivekitMembers.ts:115); remote tokens via `getSFUConfigWithOpenID` without `matrixRTCMode`: `/get_token` first, then `/sfu/get` (EC src/livekit/openIDSFU.ts:93, :118, :264). lk-jwt-service v0.7.0: a user whose homeserver is not in `LIVEKIT_FULL_ACCESS_HOMESERVERS` gets `can_publish: false`, `room_create: false` and no room is created for them (src/handler.rs:49-66, :832-870).

API addition for approval (Task 3 contract): `CoreConfig.call_service_override`.
- Behaviour changes: participants on our own SFU without an active membership are no longer played (before this task every subscribed track played); flags and the identity mapping use `sender:device_id` instead of `membershipID`.

With echo cancellation off it came through at 0.2880, and the multi-SFU levels became symmetric (0.2879 and 0.2858). So AEC3 suppressed carol's near end while loud far-end audio played, although the virtual devices have no echo path at all. This is on the human by-ear list (double talk); an option for Task 15 is AEC off by default with headphones.

## Phase 6 / Task 9 notes
- Checks run (all pass): `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -- -D warnings`; `cargo test --workspace --locked` (432 passed, 0 failed, per binary); `scripts/check-app-deps.sh` (and `livekit`); `scripts/check-core-api.sh`; `scripts/check-no-webrtc-download.sh`; `cargo deny check` (the new matrix-sdk feature changes no lock entry); against a recreated stack, `cargo test -p tessera-core -p tessera-app --locked -- --ignored --skip keyring --skip hardware_`: 28/28 (call_media 4, call_signalling 2, rtc_stack 3, and the Phase 1/2 suites); no `secret`/`derived_key` line in any gate log.
- Element Call behaviour followed (sources read at the tags in the scratchpad): key transport `ToDeviceKeyTransport` (js-sdk v43 src/matrixrtc/ToDeviceKeyTransport.ts:69 send, :149 receive, :171 validation; event type `io.element.call.encryption_keys`, src/@types/event.ts:115); rollout and rotation `RTCEncryptionManager.rolloutOutboundKey` (src/matrixrtc/RTCEncryptionManager.ts:356-448; 16-byte keys :503; `useKeyDelay` 1000 ms and grace 10 s set in `join`, :256-257; Element Call sets neither); always this manager since v43 (MatrixRTCSession.ts:626). Element Call's `MatrixKeyProvider` imports the key as HKDF material with `ratchetWindowSize: 10, keyringSize: 256` (src/e2ee/matrixKeyProvider.ts:20, :51) and uses per-participant E2EE iff the room has an encryption state event (src/e2ee/sharedKeyManagement.ts:105-114). livekit-client 2.22.3 derives with HKDF-SHA-256, salt `LKFrameEncryptionKey`, info 128 zero bytes, AES-GCM 128 (src/e2ee/utils.ts:70-115, constants.ts:38-47; failure tolerance 10). The native side, built from `webrtc-sdk/webrtc` branch `m150_release` (rust-sdks tag `webrtc-89d790b` → commit 24f7126, webrtc-sys/libwebrtc/.gclient; a branch, so the exact revision is not pinned there), derives the same (api/crypto/frame_crypto_transformer.cc:248-272), leaves 1 byte of audio frames unencrypted as livekit-client does, and encrypts with the cryptor's own key index (:494-517) while decrypting by the index in the frame trailer (:667).
- SDK APIs used: matrix-sdk feature `experimental-send-custom-to-device` (approved list, decision 4): `Client::send_encrypted_to_device` (src/encryption/mod.rs:920-957, `CollectStrategy::AllDevices`, as the widget driver); to-device handlers receive `Option<EncryptionInfo>`, `Some` only for Olm-decrypted events (src/event_handler/mod.rs:370-395); `Room::latest_encryption_state` (room/mod.rs:1124). livekit 0.9.3: `RoomOptions.encryption` (`#[non_exhaustive]`, set on a default), `E2eeOptions { encryption_type: Gcm, key_provider }`, `KeyProvider::{new, set_key}` with `KeyProviderOptions { key_derivation_algorithm: HKDF, .. }` (room/e2ee/key_provider.rs), `E2eeManager::frame_cryptors` and `FrameCryptor::set_key_index` (libwebrtc native/frame_cryptor.rs:173); the manager sets no key index itself (room/e2ee/manager.rs:111-200). ruma `Base64<Standard>` (decodes padded and unpadded; we send padded, as js-sdk's `encodeBase64`).
- Measured (`encrypted_room_calls_are_end_to_end_encrypted`): alice hears carol 0.2805, carol hears alice 0.2312 (0.3 sent); a LiveKit listener with a valid token and no keys decodes 4.5 s of audio with the tone at 0.00126-0.00821 (noise from decoding ciphertext; threshold 0.03), alice's publication `Gcm`; after carol leaves and rejoins 0.2870 / 0.2848. Passed 4 runs in a row. Mutations, all failing as expected and reverted: frame encryption off (listener hears 0.23114, publication `None`); keys not sent (members hear 0.0000); rotated key not installed, or installed without setting the encryptor's index (carol hears alice 0.0000 after rejoining).
- **Findings:** (1) js-sdk v43 creates a new key when the last other member leaves but returns before using it, so whoever left could still decrypt the remaining member until the next rotation; we use it at once. The first version here had the same gap (no rotation with nobody to send to); the rejoin test caught it only once the rotation mutation passed, and the fix was checked with that mutation. (2) LiveKit's native encryptor keeps key index 0 unless told otherwise, unlike livekit-client, which follows the latest key; without setting it, a rotation silently breaks our audio for the others. (3) The native frame cryptor logs key material (`secret`, `derived_key`) at `LS_INFO`; WebRTC release builds log nothing by default, and the gate grep found no such line, but libwebrtc info logging must never be enabled. (4) One run, between the two fixes, had neither side hearing the other after the rejoin; it did not come back in 4 later runs or in the same key state (mutation runs), so it is recorded, not explained.
- API addition for approval (Task 3 contract): `CallState::Connected { encrypted }`.
- Not covered locally: interop with Element Call's own key exchange (human item); devices without Olm sessions yet (the SDK claims one-time keys before sending); cross-signing based recipient filtering (we send to all of a member's devices, as Element Call).
- Open issues: a member whose key never arrives is silent without a UI hint; key material passes through `Vec<u8>` copies into the FFI and the SDK's JSON (`Raw`), which we cannot zeroize.
- New human items: encrypted-room interop with Element Web and Element X (folded into the interop item).

## Phase 6 / Task 10 notes
- Checks run (all pass): `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -- -D warnings`; `cargo test --workspace --locked` (437 passed, 0 failed, per binary); `scripts/check-app-deps.sh` (and `livekit`); `scripts/check-core-api.sh`; `scripts/check-no-webrtc-download.sh`; `cargo deny check`; against a recreated stack, `cargo test -p tessera-core -p tessera-app --locked -- --ignored --skip keyring --skip hardware_`: 32/32 (call_ringing 4, call_media 4, call_signalling 2, rtc_stack 3, and the Phase 1/2 suites).
- Element Call behaviour followed (matrix-js-sdk v43 and Element Call 0.26.1, read at those tags): the first member to join sends the notification (`MatrixRTCSession` `sendCallNotify`, after its own membership is sent), `ring` in a DM and `notification` elsewhere, `m.mentions { room: true }`, `m.reference` to the membership event, `lifetime` 90 s, `slot_id` `m.call#ROOM`; receiving follows `parseCallNotificationContent` and `getCallNotificationExpiry` (lifetime capped at 120 s, `sender_ts` ignored when more than 20 s ahead of the server's timestamp); declines are `org.matrix.msc4310.rtc.decline` with an `m.reference` to the notification. Notifications go out as regular events, js-sdk's fallback without sticky events (MSC4354).
- **MSC ambiguity (MSC4143 slots):** js-sdk only rings while an `org.matrix.msc4143.rtc.slot` state event for `m.call#ROOM` is open, but Element Call 0.26.1 never creates one for `m.call.member` calls, so nothing would ever ring. Here a missing slot event counts as open and a closed one stops the ring. To revisit once MSC4143 settles or Element Call starts writing slots.
- Built: `call::ringing` (content builders and the receiving rules, unit-tested), `call::watcher::CallWatch` (one per sync session: rings, declines of our notification, per-room call counts, a 2 s recheck for expiry and memberships that lapse without an event), `DeclineCall`, the notification sent by `CallSession::join` when nobody else is in the call, and `RoomSummary.is_call_room` from the SDK's `Room::is_call` (MSC3417).
- API additions for approval (Task 3 contract): `IncomingCall.ring`, `CoreEvent::CallDeclined { room_id, user_id }`, `RoomSummary.is_call_room`.
- Tests (`tests/call_ringing.rs`, alice and carol): a DM call rings carol (`ring: true`, alice as caller) and the room counts 1, answering stops the ring and counts 2; declining stops the ring and alice gets `CallDeclined` from carol, a second decline is refused; in a group room the notification is quiet (`ring: false`) and stops when alice leaves (count 0); an MSC3417 room is marked as a call room. Mutations, both failing as expected and reverted: no notification sent (3 of 4 fail; the call-room test does not depend on it), our sent notification not remembered (the declining test fails).
- **Process finding (fixed):** since Task 8, `tests/call_signalling.rs` started its cores with the system audio backend, so the agent's local gate opened the real microphone and speakers, against the rule that `hardware_*` tests are human-run. The test helper now uses the silent virtual backend, and `tests/call_settings.rs`'s hardware test asks for the system backend explicitly (folded into the Task 8 commit).
- Not covered locally: ringing with Element Web and Element X (human item); a decline from another of our own devices (handled, not tested: one account per test core); push rules for the notification event on the server.
- Open issues: `IncomingCall.intent` is always `Audio` (we do not read `m.call.intent` yet; video is out of scope); the UI to answer or decline comes with the call UI.
- New human items: ringing with Element Web and Element X.

- Checks run (all pass): `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -- -D warnings`; `cargo test --workspace --locked` (448 passed, 0 failed, per binary); `scripts/check-app-deps.sh` (and `livekit`); `scripts/check-core-api.sh`; `scripts/check-no-webrtc-download.sh`; `cargo deny check` (the new crates' licences pass); against a recreated stack, `cargo test -p tessera-core -p tessera-app --locked -- --ignored --skip keyring --skip hardware_`: 32/32. One earlier run failed `a_dm_call_rings_and_answering_stops_it`: an agent mistake, two gate runs overlapping on the same homeserver and logs (the first went on after a clippy failure). The local gate script now stops at the first failure and takes a lock; the clean rerun passed.
- APIs read in the registry before use: global-hotkey 0.8.0 (`GlobalHotKeyManager::{new, register, unregister}`, the global `GlobalHotKeyEvent::receiver`, `HotKey: FromStr`; X11 grabs with `XGrabKey` and detectable auto-repeat on its own thread polling every 50 ms, Windows `RegisterHotKey` with release polled through `GetAsyncKeyState` every 50 ms, macOS Carbon hot keys; on X11 a manager without a display fails silently, hence the session detection), ashpd 0.13.13 (`GlobalShortcuts::{create_session, bind_shortcuts, receive_activated, receive_deactivated}`, `NewShortcut::preferred_trigger`, `register_host_app`), gilrs 0.11.2 (`Gilrs::next_event_blocking`, `EventType::{ButtonPressed, ButtonReleased, Disconnected, Dropped}`).
- Built: `tessera-ptt` (`PttService`, `PttConfig`, `PttEvent`, `KeyboardBackend`, `KeyBinding`, `GamepadButton`), the `ptt_probe` example, and `AudioSettings.push_to_talk { key, gamepad_button }` in the core (API addition for approval; old settings files load with no bindings). The app's `bridge::Pending` now boxes its command, since the larger settings value made it trip clippy's `result_large_err`.
- Tests: 9 unit tests in `tessera-ptt` and 2 in the core settings; the settings integration test saves and restores the bindings. Mutations, failing as expected and reverted: reporting every press instead of merging (2 tests fail), not letting go on drop (1 fails).
- New dependencies (approved list, decision 4): global-hotkey =0.8.0, gilrs =0.11.2, ashpd =0.13.13 (Linux; `tokio`, `global_shortcuts`). New lock entries: ashpd, crossbeam-channel, gilrs, gilrs-core, global-hotkey, inotify, inotify-sys, keyboard-types, libudev-sys, objc2-io-kit, vec_map. gilrs needs libudev at build time on Linux: `libudev-dev` added to the CI package lines (Arch/SteamOS: `systemd-libs`, already installed).
- **Limitations:** (1) a global-hotkey binding grabs the key, so a plain letter would stop typing that letter everywhere; the UI should suggest a modifier combination or an unused key (F13–F24, a mouse/keyboard macro key). A non-grabbing listener needs low-level hooks per OS (Windows `WH_KEYBOARD_LL`, macOS event taps with the Input Monitoring permission, X11 XInput2 raw events); recorded, not planned. (2) No modifier-only or mouse-button bindings (global-hotkey cannot). (3) The portal's dialog and its key choice belong to the compositor; GNOME may refuse a host app whose ID registration failed. (4) gilrs reads `/dev/input`: inside Flatpak or without access to the input devices, pads may not be visible.
- Not in this task: the app starting the service from the settings and forwarding `Transmit` comes with the call UI; the CLI's `/ptt` is Task 12. The agent did not run the probe: binding a global key or opening the portal dialog on the human's desktop is a human check.

- Tests: 4 unit tests in `pw/graph.rs`; 4 tests against a private PipeWire daemon (`crates/audio/tests/pipewire.rs`, part of the local gate): the list leaves out streams and marks defaults; hot-plug reported at once by the watcher; tone loopback 0.3000 for 0.3; a game's sound sent at −20.6 dBFS with the call's own reference and −73.3 dBFS with the system mix (52.7 dB less). Mutations, failing as expected and reverted: streams classed as devices (the list test fails, our own playback stream shows up as a speaker); the voice engine ignoring the reference (0.0 dB less). The Task 4 output test (300 ms of silence, default output) passes on the real PipeWire through the new client; the microphone was not opened.
- The private daemon: `pipewire -c <test.conf>` with null-audio-sink nodes (`Audio/Sink`, `Audio/Source/Virtual`), the dummy driver and the default metadata, in a short temp dir (a socket path must fit in 108 bytes); a client config with `adapter.auto-port-config` (no session manager configures ports there); links made with `pw-link`. Each test re-runs itself in a child pointed at the daemon, so no environment is changed in a running test process.
- Findings: (1) without a session manager, stream and device ports only exist with `adapter.auto-port-config`; real systems get them from WirePlumber. (2) Links between lingering nodes outlive a test step (`pw-link` then says "File exists"). (3) On Linux nothing resamples on our side any more: PipeWire's adapter converts to 48 kHz mono.
- Open issues: the echo reference follows the call's output device; audio played on another device is not cancelled. Not measured yet: CPU of the new path against cpal's (Task 15); SteamOS and Flatpak (the PipeWire socket inside a sandbox).
- New human items: the PipeWire client checks (folded into the list).

## Phase 6 / Task 13b notes (part 3, TensorRT)
- Checks run: fmt, clippy (`--all-features`), ONNX Runtime tests 3/3 with CUDA and TensorRT each (from an empty engine cache and from a cached engine, twice each), core GPU test 1/1 from an empty cache (TensorRT chosen); full gate below.
- APIs read before use (ort 2.0.0-rc.13 source): `ep::TensorRT::{with_engine_cache, with_engine_cache_path, with_timing_cache, with_timing_cache_path, with_cuda_graph}`, `with_execution_providers` with two providers (TensorRT, then CUDA), `ep::{CUDA, TensorRT}::with_compute_stream` (unsafe: the stream must outlive the session), `RunOptions::disable_device_sync` (`disable_synchronize_execution_providers`), `Session::run_binding_with_options`. TensorRT version from the provider's `NEEDED` entries (`libnvinfer.so.10`, `libnvonnxparser.so.10`); NVIDIA's wheel loader (`tensorrt_libs/__init__.py`) for what to preload.
- Built: `scripts/fetch-tensorrt.sh` + `scripts/tensorrt.sha256` (shellcheck clean); `tessera_audio::onnx::default_tensorrt_dir`, TensorRT preload, engine/timing caches, CUDA fallback provider; the loop runs TensorRT with device-resident state and its CUDA graph; `dfn_profile trt`; the GPU tests cover each backend the runtime has; the core tries TensorRT first.
- Measured: TensorRT 0.135 ms per frame and about 37 µs of CPU (CUDA 0.35 ms and 58 µs, CPU 0.27 ms and 267 µs; CPU per frame from every thread's kernel run time during the frame loop); first engine build about 20 s, cached loads 0.2–0.4 s; outputs 86–88 dB from the CPU's across builds (CUDA 91 dB).
- **Race found and fixed (2026-10-03, found by the Task 15 gate):** the ONNX Runtime GPU tests failed every run (TensorRT 26–40 dB from the CPU, frame-by-frame differences of 3–6); not the new x86-64-v3 flags (a baseline build failed the same) nor the engine cache (a fresh build too). Our synchronous `cudaMemcpy` calls on the default stream were not ordered with TensorRT's replay on its own stream. Confirmed by a device-wide sync around the run (exact again); fixed by running the providers on a CUDA stream of ours (`ep::{CUDA, TensorRT}::with_compute_stream`) with every copy queued on it (`cudaMemcpyAsync`) and one `cudaStreamSynchronize` per frame, ONNX Runtime's own wait off (`RunOptions::disable_device_sync`). The earlier "TF32 variance" (40–88 dB) and the wrong replays after an engine build were this race: the warm-up workaround is removed (fresh and cached engines pass without it), and the test bar is 60 dB for CUDA and TensorRT. Checks: ONNX Runtime tests 3/3 in each of 11 runs, 3 of them from a fresh engine (one without the warm-up). Wait orders measured: one wait at the end costs least. `dfn_profile` now prints CPU per frame.
- Open: the first build blocks the GPU load for about 20 s (RNNoise runs meanwhile; the UI should say "preparing the GPU"); old engines stay in the cache when the model changes (9 MB each); TensorRT 11 needs a newer ONNX Runtime (version question, rule 3).
- Gate: `encrypted_room_calls_are_end_to_end_encrypted` (Task 9) failed once in the gate run: after carol rejoined, alice heard nothing from her after the fixed 6 s wait (0.0000; no partial audio). It then passed 3/3 alone, and 6/6 with the wait replaced by polling up to 20 s (both directions back within 3 s every time); the polling is folded into the Task 9 commit and prints how long the rejoin took. Not reproduced since: if it recurs, the printed time says whether the audio comes back late or never (a key-rotation race would be a Task 9 bug).
- New human items: none beyond the GPU-by-ear item (it now runs TensorRT when fetched).

## Phase 6 / Task 13b notes (part 4, WebGPU)
- Checks run: fmt, clippy (`--all-features`), ONNX Runtime tests 3/3 with CUDA, TensorRT and WebGPU (WebGPU through Vulkan on the RTX 5090), `dfn_profile webgpu`; full gate below.
- APIs read before use (ort 2.0.0-rc.13 source): `Environment::{register_ep_library, devices}` (`api-22`, within our `api-28`), `Device::ep`, `SessionBuilder::with_devices` (options prefixed with the provider's name), `ExecutionProviderLibrary` (no `Drop`: stays registered), `memory::AllocationDevice::WEBGPU_BUFFER`, `IoBinding::bind_output` (`bind_output_mut` is private: the run's outputs are the bound buffers), `SessionOutputs::get`, `SessionBuilder::with_profiling`, `ort::api().CopyTensors` (ort-sys `api-23`) with `Error::result_from_status` and `AsPointer`; `ort`'s own tensor copy does not cover WebGPU memory. Plugin 0.4.0: README and `__init__.py` from the wheel (one device per session, name `WebGpuExecutionProvider`), option names from its library (`ep.webgpuexecutionprovider.*`).
- Built: `scripts/fetch-webgpu-ep.sh` + `scripts/webgpu-ep.sha256` (shellcheck not installed here; `bash -n` clean); `Accelerator::WebGpu`, registration at load, `default_webgpu_dir`, `WEBGPU_EP_VERSION`; the WebGPU stateful loop (state in GPU buffers, captured graph, `CopyTensors`); `OnnxLoop::uses_cuda_graphs` renamed `replays_a_graph` (covers both); `dfn_profile webgpu`; WebGPU in both GPU tests; core `GpuBackend::WebGpu`, tried after TensorRT and CUDA; `TESSERA_ONNX_PROFILE` (ONNX Runtime's per-node trace) and `TESSERA_WEBGPU_EP_OPTIONS` (provider options) for profiling on other machines.
- Measured (RTX 5090, Vulkan): 0.59 ms per frame (p99 1.6 ms) and 0.42 ms of CPU, more CPU than the CPU path (0.26 ms) on this machine; without graph capture 1.72 ms and 2.2 ms. Output 133 dB from the CPU graphs; frame by frame within 1.3e-5. Where the CPU goes, and what was tried: the plan (Task 13b, WebGPU). In short: the NVIDIA driver spins through the fence wait (~140 µs), the plugin allocates and frees a staging buffer per copy (~175 µs), and a captured graph is re-encoded on each replay (~200 µs; WebGPU has no reusable command buffers). Not tuned further without AMD numbers.
- **Finding, fixed in the export:** the plugin computes the one non-depthwise grouped convolution (`df_convp`, 2 groups) wrongly on some inputs (single coefficients off by up to 0.12, deterministic; isolated with `forceCpuNodeNames`). `scripts/export-deepfilternet-stateful.py` now writes such convolutions ungrouped with block-diagonal weights (same arithmetic; within 1e-6 of upstream's graphs); `scripts/models.sha256` updated (re-export reproducible). CUDA, TensorRT and the CPU agree as before.
- Open: WebGPU untested on AMD, Intel, Windows, macOS and aarch64; the staging-buffer churn and per-replay encoding are upstream's (ONNX Runtime's WebGPU provider, Dawn); `maxNumPendingDispatches` trades CPU for latency, to decide on AMD numbers.
- New human items: Galep's AMD check (below); approvals for the plugin download and `GpuBackend::WebGpu`; whether to report the grouped-convolution bug upstream.

## Phase 6 / Task 15 notes (part 1, profiling on x86_64)
- Built: `crates/audio/examples/voice_profile.rs` (per-stage cost and allocations, allocation callers with `VOICE_PROFILE_TRACE`, one configuration alone for `perf`); `perf` installed (user space).
- Measured (per 10 ms frame, desktop): default chain 97 µs (p99 111 µs), about 1 % of a core, 51.5 allocations; DeepFilterNet3 on the CPU 270 µs and 1028 allocations; mixing 0.8 µs per remote voice. Details and the ranked candidates in the plan (Task 15).
- No bottleneck in the default path. Candidates, none applied (decisions for the human): x86-64-v3 builds (−17 %); AGC2 using RNNoise's voice probability (−12 %, needs sonora upstream); ONNX Runtime CPU for DeepFilterNet3 (same speed, 19 allocations instead of 1028, ships ONNX Runtime everywhere); upstream reports for AGC2's allocations.
- Open: aarch64 numbers (Frame or CI arm runner), Opus cost, lateness under load and real-time priority (rest of Task 15).
- GPU outside modern NVIDIA (human decision 2026-10-03): ONNX Runtime's WebGPU provider (Vulkan through Dawn on Linux, Direct3D 12 on Windows), planned as Task 13b part 4; see the plan's research note for what to measure first.
- Part 3, real-time priority and wake-ups (Linux, PipeWire): the processing thread no longer polls (it slept 2 ms whenever a frame was incomplete); the microphone's callback wakes it. It is created through PipeWire's real-time module (its thread utilities on our context) and asks for priority one under PipeWire's data loops, read back from the kernel and reported in `IoInfo::realtime` and the core's log (`TESSERA_AUDIO_REALTIME=0` turns it off). Lateness counters `IoStats::{wakes, late_wakes, max_wake_us}`. Tests (PipeWire suite, ignored, private daemon; the test daemon and client now load the real-time module as PipeWire's own configs do): under 64 busy threads for 5 s, real-time 0 of ~500 wakes over 1 ms and worst 98–106 µs (priority 82 by limits; 19 through rtkit with `ulimit -r 0`); normal priority 97–156 over 1 ms, worst 3.2–4.4 ms; idle worst 94 µs. Also checked by hand: without the module the thread runs, refused with the reason. APIs read before use: libpipewire 1.6.9's `thread.h` (exported functions), `module-rt.c` (rtkit lists only threads it created; the grant is asynchronous; utilities on the context via `pw_context_set_object`), `pipewire` 0.10.1 `ContextRc::as_raw_ptr`, `PropertiesBox::insert`, `pw_sys::pw_context_get_object`, `spa_sys::{SPA_TYPE_INTERFACE_ThreadUtils, spa_thread_utils, spa_thread_utils_methods}`; rtrb 0.4.0 `Producer::{buffer, slots}`.
- **Ringing flake found by the part 3 gate (2026-10-04), fixed in the Task 4 and Task 10 code:** `call_ringing` failed twice in a row (a different encrypted-DM test each time; carol never got the ring). Three races, each found under load (busy threads on all cores; the tests pass unloaded) and seen in debug logs (room IDs only): (1) the sync service brings room events and to-device keys on separate connections, and the SDK calls event handlers once, with what it could decrypt then (`Client::handle_sync_timeline_events`), so a notification that came before its room key was lost. The watcher now holds recent undecryptable events (younger than the longest ring, at most 64) and tries them again off the sync path, once at once and then when keys for their Megolm session arrive (`Encryption::room_keys_received_stream`, `Room::decrypt_event`); a first version that retried inside the sync handler and held old history slowed every sync and was replaced. (2) A notification handled before the caller's membership reached the store was dropped as "no call"; it now waits (until it expires) and rings when its sender's membership arrives. (3) The delayed leave (18 s) was restarted only once the join finished; a join slower than that (the SFU connection under load) let the server send our leave, so the other side never saw us. Restarts now start as soon as the leave is scheduled. Also: a notification is handled once (by event ID, until it expires), since a sync that starts over sends recent events again. Checks: under 28 busy threads, 20 of 20 runs of both DM tests passed (5 rings recovered by the held path; with only the first fix, 2 of 16 runs failed, one on each of the other races); unloaded 3 of 3. Note for these tests: each run leaves carol in two more encrypted DMs whose history new devices cannot decrypt, so loops must reset the users (`scripts/create-test-users.sh`) as the gate does. Follow-up (human-approved 2026-10-04): after a stall longer than 18 s the server sent our leave and we stayed invisible. Now, as matrix-js-sdk v43's `MembershipManager` ("missing own membership: force re-join", read at that tag), when our membership disappears from the room state after we had seen it, we join again from scratch: the old delayed leave cancelled (`UpdateAction::Cancel`; left alone it would fire and remove the new membership), a new one scheduled, a fresh membership sent (no inherited `created_ts`, as js-sdk). No key rotation is needed: neither side drops a member's keys when it leaves, and the others rotate theirs and share them with us as with any rejoining device. One task (`keep_membership`) now restarts the delayed leave, refreshes the validity and rejoins. That merge fixed a latent bug: the refresh slept the whole accumulated validity each time, so the second refresh came at about 11 h 40 for a membership valid until 8 h. Test `call_signalling::a_removed_membership_is_restored_and_stays` (the membership emptied by another client, as the delayed leave does: it comes back and is still there after 25 s, then leaving removes it) fails on the old code and passes; the signalling suite 3/3. The first gate after it failed once in `call_media::alices_tone_reaches_carols_speakers_and_mute_stops_it` (tone at 0.049, bar 0.05, usually 0.27–0.29), right after a clean build and a recreated stack; the test passed 6 runs after, and the next full gate passed. Not twice in a row; recorded in case it recurs. (It recurred twice in a row in part 4; cause found and fixed there: call audio starved on the core's runtime.)
- **Part 4 (2026-10-04): Windows and macOS priority, lateness in `AudioStats`, Opus cost.** Details and numbers in the plan (Task 15, part 4).
  - Windows and macOS: `audio_thread_priority` =0.35.1 (human choice: Mozilla's crate over our own FFI; MPL-2.0, allowed by deny.toml) raises the processing thread (MMCSS "Audio" task; macOS time-constraint policy), reported as `Realtime::GrantedBy`; cpal's `realtime` feature on Windows for its WASAPI threads. Compile-checked and clippy-clean for Windows and macOS (cross, with clang); not run. `set_echo_reference` is Linux-only now (it warned as unused elsewhere, which `-D warnings` fails).
  - `AudioStats` (API addition, for approval): `late_wakes`, `max_wake_us`, `capture_overruns`, `playback_underruns`, per second; `IoCounters::take_recent_max_wake_us` (unit test). PipeWire load tests: worst wake while loaded 8–18 µs at real-time priority, 3.1–4.3 ms without.
  - Opus (`cargo run --release -p tessera-core --example opus_profile`, libwebrtc's Opus with WebRTC's settings): 64 kb/s complexity 9: voice 20.6 µs per 10 ms, room noise 68 µs, digital silence 30.6 µs (6.7 µs with FTZ); 32 kb/s: 66–80 µs; complexity 5 at 32 kb/s: 26–37 µs; decoding 2–10 µs per voice. In a call the encoder costs 50–55 µs talking and 220–345 µs silent or muted (denormals in Opus's `tone_lpc`; WebRTC's encoder thread does not flush them). The gap to the benchmark on silence is not explained yet.
  - **Call audio gaps, found and fixed (in the Task 8b commit):** the tasks moving audio between LiveKit and our engine ran on the core's runtime; LiveKit drops decoded audio after 100 ms and sends silence when its 50 ms microphone queue runs dry, so stalls were heard as gaps (bursts of 10 frames every 200 ms, seen with a probe; the `call_media` "0.049 blip", which failed twice in a row this time). They now run on their own thread (`tessera-call-audio`): the tone arrives at 0.284–0.286 in 10 of 10 runs, no gaps. That exposed a speaking bug: LiveKit's `ActiveSpeakersChanged` lists only who started, and we replaced the whole set with it, so a member still talking was shown silent when another began (`a_participant_without_a_membership_is_not_played` then failed 3 of 3, its intruder taking over); the set is now read from each participant's own flag. The tests' "heard" bar is 0.15 (`HEARD`, was 0.05). Checks: `call_media` 4/4 in 5 of 5 runs. Also fixed (Task 14 commit): the PipeWire tests' private daemons shared one folder per process, so tests run in parallel broke each other; all 6 now pass in parallel.
  - **Why silence costs more in a call (explained 2026-10-04):** the encoder gets exact digital silence (all 654 frames of a silent microphone were zeros, dumped in the call test) with WebRTC's mono voice settings (negotiated `minptime=10;usedtx=1;useinbandfec=1`, no `stereo=1`); no Opus setting reproduced the call's cost on its own (worst 85 µs). The difference is the core: this i9-14900K has P- and E-cores, and WebRTC's encoder threads ran on E-cores in all 451 samples (`perf --sample-cpu`). On digital silence Opus's `tone_lpc` (CELT's tone detector) underflows, and on an E-core each underflow is a slow microcode assist: 64 kb/s silence 210 µs per 10 ms on an E-core against 32 µs on a P-core (a tone: 27 against 17 µs). With FTZ/DAZ on the E-core: silence after speech 311 → 10 µs, pure silence 302 → 95 µs. The newest Opus (1.6.1, the system's) is the same (227 µs on an E-core); libwebrtc's copy reports no version. WebRTC's encoder thread does not flush denormals, and muting the track (`LocalAudioTrack::mute`, which we call) does not stop it encoding zeros. Not fixable cheaply on our side: a ±1 LSB dither instead of zeros halves the E-core cost but stops DTX (every packet sent) and costs more on a P-core (66 against 31 µs). Our own processing thread ran on E-cores about 70 % of the time in the same run (it flushes denormals, so only the slower core applies): per-stage numbers measured so far are P-core numbers. No Opus issue on GitHub covers it (searched: denormal, subnormal, tone_lpc, silence). The Frame (aarch64) may not have the penalty; `opus_profile` measures it there.
  - Open: Frame defaults (bitrate, complexity, silence), aarch64 numbers, E-core numbers for the voice chain.
- x86-64-v3 (human decision 2026-10-03: "a free improvement for compatible CPUs", i9-14900K and Ryzen 7 9800X3D both have it): `.cargo/config.toml` adds `-C target-cpu=x86-64-v3` for x86_64 Linux; default chain 97 → 80.7 µs per frame, same allocations. `tessera_core::cpu_level`: an `.init_array` constructor in assembly (CPUID leaves 1, 7 and 0x8000_0001, and XCR0) exits with a message on a CPU without the level, before any Rust code (which the compiler may build with those instructions) runs; both binaries call `tessera_core::require_cpu_level()` so the constructor is linked. Checked: pointed at AVX-512F (missing on the i9-14900K) it printed the message and exited 1; with the real bits it passes. Windows and macOS x86_64 stay baseline for now (no tested check there). Note: RUSTFLAGS in the environment replaces the config's flags.

## Phase 6 / Task 16 notes (part 1, cleaning other voices on the CPU)
- Decided 2026-10-04 (human): the "already clean" signal is read from LiveKit's per-track audio features (filled by livekit-client: `TF_NOISE_SUPPRESSION` from the browser's suppressor, `TF_ENHANCED_NOISE_CANCELLATION` from Krisp; Element Call 0.26.1 turns the browser's on by default and has no Krisp, both read at their sources) and announced by us as a LiveKit participant attribute (LiveKit's Rust SDK 0.9.3 reads track features but cannot set them: `AddTrackRequest` only gets the pre-connect flag, no `UpdateLocalAudioTrack`). Build order: CPU first (this part), then the signal and policy, then batched GPU.
- Built: `DeepFilterModel` (tessera-audio): the three networks compiled once (tract's runnable plans are shared, `spawn` takes `&Arc<Self>`, read in tract-core 0.23.8), one cheap filter state per voice; a filter is bit-identical whether or not another runs beside it (test). The voice engine cleans each remote source that has a cleaner before its volume and the mix (`VoiceControl::set_source_cleaner`, `set_cleaner_attenuation`); replaced, removed and failing cleaners go back to the control side to be dropped (as the outgoing enhancer); a failing one leaves the voice as it came and drops its `CleanedFlag` (tests).
- Core (`crates/core/src/call/cleaning.rs`): one manager per call, shared by its SFU connections (one engine). It decides which voices are cleaned (none while off; per-person never skipped; always first, then the earliest to join; at most `limits::CLEANED_VOICES` = 4; unit tests), loads the model once per call and each filter off the audio thread, drops stale filters off it too, and publishes each participant's `cleaning` status (off, starting, cleaned, skipped: never, limit, unavailable). A filter that fails on the audio thread is noticed within a second. Race found by the new test under load (2 of 3 runs failed): the failure watch first ticked at once and read a just-handed-over filter's flag before the engine plugged it in; it now waits a second after the hand-over (4 of 4 runs after).
- Cost (`voice_profile`, per 10 ms frame, default chain, 4 voices): none cleaned 82 µs on a performance core (157 on an efficiency core); 1 cleaned 387 (717); 4 cleaned 1.28 ms (2.31 ms); 8 cleaned 2.47 ms (4.48 ms); about 1030 allocations per cleaned voice per frame (tract's, as Task 13). Hence the limit of 4 (under a quarter of the frame on an efficiency core); the Frame's limit comes from its own numbers.
- API additions (for approval): `AudioSettings::{remote_cleaning: RemoteCleaning { enabled, attenuation_limit_db }, user_cleaning: BTreeMap<UserId, VoiceCleaning>}` (`VoiceCleaning::{Automatic, Always, Never}`, automatic not stored), `Command::SetUserCleaning`, `Participant::cleaning: CleaningStatus` (`Off`, `Starting`, `Cleaned`, `Skipped(SkipReason::{Never, Limit, Unavailable})`), `limits::CLEANED_VOICES`. CLI: `/clean [on|off]`, `/clean limit <dB>`, `/clean <n|user> always|never|auto`; `/who` shows each status, `/audio` the switch.
- Checks: test `call_media::remote_voices_are_cleaned_when_asked` (carol's steady tone, which DeepFilterNet takes for noise, reaches alice at 0.287 plain, 0.0001–0.0002 cleaned, 0.289 set to never; the status follows); the call tests 5/5 in 4 runs; unit tests for the decisions, validation and the CLI.
- Open after part 1: the skip signal and policy (part 2), batched GPU cleaning (part 3), the loudest voices first, resting silent voices' filters, the Frame's limit.
- **Part 1b (2026-10-04, human request): silent voices rest, the loudest talkers are cleaned.** The engine now picks, every frame, which filters run: those of voices that talk (a voice under about -60 dBFS for 300 ms rests its filter and plays at the filter's floor, its attenuation limit; the filter resumes on its first louder frame, its state as it was, no warm-up), at most `CLEANED_VOICES` (4) at once: voices set to always first, then those already running (kept while they talk, no flapping), then the loudest (energy smoothed over about 100 ms). A talking voice over that plays as it came (`SourceCleaning::OverLimit`, status `Skipped(Limit)`). The core gives a filter to every eligible voice, up to `CLEANING_FILTERS` (16) per call (the earliest to join keep theirs), tells the engine the priorities, and republishes the statuses once a second. A muted Tessera voice decodes to exact digital silence at the receiver (measured: -240 dBFS, peak 0), so its filter rests; a browser client sending room noise above -60 dBFS keeps it running, when it is useful. Cost (per 10 ms, performance core / efficiency core): 8 cleaned all talking 1.28 / 2.33 ms (was 2.47 / 4.48: four run), 8 cleaned with 2 talking 0.69 / 1.25 ms, 16 cleaned with 1 talking 0.41 / 0.73 ms. Tests: a quiet voice rests after 300 ms, plays at the floor (0 at 100 dB, -20 dB at 20) and is cleaned from its first loud frame; with one slot the louder talker gets it, keeps it while it talks, a priority voice takes it at once, a resting voice does not count; the call tests 5/5 twice.

## Phase 6 known limitations
- Denormal guard (decision 7, human-approved 2026-10-02, pre-production): `crates/audio/src/denormals.rs` is the one audited `unsafe` function. It sets FTZ/DAZ (x86_64) or FZ (aarch64) on the audio processing thread, because sonora 0.2.0 AEC3 slows down on a silent far end after speech (upstream issue #34; measured 5.5× here, see Task 5). Remove it once a sonora release with upstream PR #38 ships (version bump asked first, rule 3).
- sonora 0.2.0 allocates about 40 times per 10 ms frame in AEC3 (upstream #39/#40). The processing thread is not a device callback, so this is tolerable, but not real-time clean until upstream ships the fix.

Also once with `TESSERA_WEBGPU_EP_OPTIONS="maxNumPendingDispatches=512"`. Then by ear in a call with DeepFilterNet on the GPU.
- [x] Approve the Task 13b part 4 additions: fetching Microsoft's `onnxruntime-ep-webgpu` 0.4.0 wheel from PyPI (MIT; third-party notices copied beside the library) and `GpuBackend::WebGpu` (API addition, Task 3 contract). Approved 2026-10-04.
- [x] Post the WebGPU grouped-convolution report to microsoft/onnxruntime (decided 2026-10-04; the agent drafted the text). Posted by the human 2026-10-04: https://github.com/microsoft/onnxruntime/issues/33086. No existing issue or pull request covers it (related, different: #32928, #29651, #32478). Cause, from main's source: for a channels-last grouped Conv, `conv.cc` sizes the vector width from the total output channels, while `grouped_conv.cc` derives the group once per vector. A vector spanning two groups reads the first group's inputs whenever `output_channels_per_group % components != 0` (`df_convp`: 10 outputs, 2 groups, 2 components). `preferredLayout=NCHW` is correct. JSEP's `conv-grouped.ts` has the same logic (not tested).
- [ ] GPU enhancement (Task 13b): each offered backend on real hardware (NVIDIA and Intel CPU on the human's machine; AMD CPU and GPU on Galep's: Ryzen 7 7800X3D and Radeon RX 6950 XT, for the WebGPU/Vulkan path of Task 13b part 4, with the same latency and CPU numbers as CUDA, and the x86-64-v3 build on Zen 4), by ear and with the recorded latency; the fallback when the GPU is busy with a game.
